# Beat Detector via Threshold | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#)

On this page

Beat Detector via Threshold
===========================

##### tip

[BeatEdit for After Effects](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-after-effects/)
 has a very different, powerful and in particular very easy to use approach to animate properties in sync with music.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/beat_detector_via_threshold_0-c9ee099f3d48edd5336ae08132f4886f.png)

![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)

Beat Detection iExpressions can be applied to properties which represent the audio amplitude of some music (i.e. properties which you created with the function 'convert audio into keyframes' of the keyframe assistent). They can detect the beats in the music such that you can animate various properties according to the music. The beat detectors just recognize the beat and don't do any animation, yet. You can animate properties using other iExpressions (like the 'Change on Beat 1D') which are then linked to the beat detector. This setup allows to animate several properties according to the same beat. The 'Beat Detector via Threshold' detects all parts of the audio as beat, whose audio amplitude is inbetween 'Min Value' and 'Max Value'. This beat detector is hence best suitable to detect parts of the music whose loudness significantly differs from the remaining audio. It can also be used to detect silence (for example for automatic lip sync animation which should only be active when there is no silence).

*   ##### Min Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#min-amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes the minimal audio amplitude of a beat. Everything that has an audio amplitude in between Min Amplitude and Max Amplitude is considered as a beat.
    
*   ##### Max Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#max-amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes the maximal audio amplitude of a beat. Everything that has an audio amplitude in between Min Amplitude and Max Amplitude is considered as a beat.
    
*   ##### Smooth (Num Frames)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#smooth-num-frames "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to do not consider the audio amplitude of the current frame, but the average of the sourrounding frames. Large values hence allow to identify parts of the music as beats which have a characteristic (average) volume, wheras small values allow to react to quick changes in the audio amplitude.
    
*   ##### Fade In/Out[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#fade-inout "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Fade In Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#fade-in-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the number of frames over which the beat is faded in. If this is set to 0, the iExpression abruptly jumps on each beat from 0 to 100. If it is set to 10, the value starts already 10 frames earlier to slowly increase from 0 to 100.
    
    ##### Fade Out Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#fade-out-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the number of frames over which the beat is faded out. If this is set to 0, the iExpression abruptly jumps after each beat from 100 to 0. If it is set to 10, the value slowley decreases over 10 frames from 100 to 0.
    
    ##### Fade Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#fade-type "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines whether the beat signal fades in or out in a linear or exponential fashion.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

#### Create a Kick Drum Kinetic Type Animation[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-threshold#create-a-kick-drum-kinetic-type-animation "Direct link to heading")

##### tip

[![Create a Kick Drum Kinetic Type Animation](<Base64-Image-Removed>)](http://aescripts.com/create-a-kick-drum-kinetic-type-animation/)

[This tutorial](http://aescripts.com/create-a-kick-drum-kinetic-type-animation/)
 by Brad Magnus shows how to use iExpressions and 3D Extruder to make a sound syncronized kinetic type animation.

# Beat Switch | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-switch#)

On this page

Beat Switch
===========

##### tip

[BeatEdit for After Effects](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-after-effects/)
 has a very different, powerful and in particular very easy to use approach to animate properties in sync with music.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-11-17_11.48.18-16e6fb41fff1d9a6fe4e9eac70af9cb3.png)

The Beat Switch iExpressions allow a property to react to more than one beat slider simultaneously (say one with the [Beat Detector from Marker](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-marker)
 and one with the [Beat Detector Automatic](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic)
). The reaction to the beat sliders can be completely different for each of them. In particular, the reaction to each single beat signal can again be controlled by a separate, individual iExpression.

The Beat Switch expressions are pretty advanced and you should be familiar with the different _Change on Beat_ and _Beat Detector_ iExpressions, before you start using them. If you want to react on more than one beat signal but react on all of them in exactly the same way, better use the [Beat Mixer> iExpression.](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer)

There exist sevaral _Beat Switch_ iExpressions, namely one of 1D, 2D, 3D properties, Texts and Colors, respectively.

### Usage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-switch#usage "Direct link to heading")

Let's say you want to perform two different moves with a layer - one on each marker and one on each beat detected by the Beat Detector Automatic. Then you first create two beat sliders using the [Beat Detector from Marker](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-marker)
 and the [Beat Detector Automatic](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic)
, respectively.

Now you create two copies of your layer and animate the position of both of them using the two beat sliders with the [Change on Beat 2D](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/change-beat)
 iExpressions (or animate other properties using other [Change on Beat](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/change-beat)
 iExpressions). So now you have two copies of the layer, one doing some moves based on the first beat signal and one doing some moves based on the second beat signal. We combine them now using the _Beat Switch 2D_ iExpression. We first connecting the beat sliders to the respective parameters in the iExpression. Then in the "Value on Beat" parameters than show up after you connected the beat sliders, you can link the animations that belong to the first and second beat signal, respectively, i.e. the position of the first and second copy of your layer.

Finally you make the two copies of your layer invisible and create a third, visible copy to whose position you apply the _Beat Switch 2D_.

Now the two invisible layers move based on only the first beat signal (the markers in this case) and only the second beat signal (Beat Detector Automatic), respectively, but the visible copy combines both animations and moves based on both beat signals.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Usage](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-switch#usage)

# Beat Detector from bpm | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-bpm/#)

Beat Detector from bpm
======================

##### tip

[BeatEdit for After Effects](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-after-effects/)
 has a very different, powerful and in particular very easy to use approach to animate properties in sync with music.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/beat_detector_from_bpm_0-0c898bd58fbcc4d5a05b75a6d88493f3.png)

Beat Detection iExpressions can be applied to properties which represent the audio amplitude of some music (i.e. properties which you created with the function 'convert audio into keyframes' of the keyframe assistent). They can detect the beats in the music such that you can animate various properties according to the music. The beat detectors just recognize the beat and don't do any animation, yet. You can animate properties using other iExpressions (like the 'Change on Beat 1D') which are then linked to the beat detector. This setup allows to animate several properties according to the same beat. To use the 'Beat Detector from bpm', you need to know the beats per minute (bpm) of the music. The bpm can also be determined with the mamoworld tool Beat Assistant. Then you simply specify one sample frame where a beat occurs and the iExpression generates a regular pattern of beat signals according to the BPM.

*   ##### Beats per Minute (bpm)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-bpm/#beats-per-minute-bpm "Direct link to heading")
    
    how many beats per minute the music has (if you don't know it, use mamoworld Beat Assistant to determine it)
    
*   ##### Example Beat Frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-bpm/#example-beat-frame "Direct link to heading")
    
    specifies one example frame, at which the beat occurs. The iExpression tries to detect all other occurences of the beat automatically.
    
*   ##### Fade In/Out[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-bpm/#fade-inout "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Fade In Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-bpm/#fade-in-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the number of frames over which the beat is faded in. If this is set to 0, the iExpression abruptly jumps on each beat from 0 to 100. If it is set to 10, the value starts already 10 frames earlier to slowly increase from 0 to 100.
    
    ##### Fade Out Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-bpm/#fade-out-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the number of frames over which the beat is faded out. If this is set to 0, the iExpression abruptly jumps after each beat from 100 to 0. If it is set to 10, the value slowley decreases over 10 frames from 100 to 0.
    
    ##### Fade Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-bpm/#fade-type "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines whether the beat signal fades in or out in a linear or exponential fashion.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Beat Detector Automatic | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#)

On this page

Beat Detector Automatic
=======================

##### tip

[BeatEdit for After Effects](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-after-effects/)
 has a very different, powerful and in particular very easy to use approach to animate properties in sync with music.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/beat_detector_automatic_0-3ce67e29bdaa00c714c5107579d04896.png)

![Beat Detector Automatic expression](<Base64-Image-Removed>)

Beat Detection iExpressions can be applied to properties which represent the audio amplitude of some music (i.e. properties which you created with the function 'convert audio into keyframes' of the keyframe assistent). They can detect the beats in the music such that you can animate various properties according to the music. The beat detectors just recognize the beat and don't do any animation, yet. You can animate properties using other iExpressions (like the 'Change on Beat 1D') which are then linked to the beat detector. This setup allows to animate several properties according to the same beat. To use the beat detector automatic, you just have to provide one example frame where the music contains a beat and the iExpression automatically searches for all other places where the wave form looks very similar. Theses are then also considered to be beats. This works in particular well, if the beats are clearly visible in the wave form of the audio.

*   ##### Example Beat Frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#example-beat-frame "Direct link to heading")
    
    specifies one example frame, at which the beat occurs. The iExpression tries to detect all other occurences of the beat automatically.
    
*   ##### Tolerance (%)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#tolerance- "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines how similar other parts must be to the given example beat in order to be identified as beat, too. The lower this number, the less beats are detected. A tolerance of 0 means that other parts are identified as beats only, if they have exactly the same audio amplitude as the example beat.
    
*   ##### Fade In/Out[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#fade-inout "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Fade In Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#fade-in-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the number of frames over which the beat is faded in. If this is set to 0, the iExpression abruptly jumps on each beat from 0 to 100. If it is set to 10, the value starts already 10 frames earlier to slowly increase from 0 to 100.
    
    ##### Fade Out Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#fade-out-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the number of frames over which the beat is faded out. If this is set to 0, the iExpression abruptly jumps after each beat from 100 to 0. If it is set to 10, the value slowley decreases over 10 frames from 100 to 0.
    
    ##### Fade Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#fade-type "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines whether the beat signal fades in or out in a linear or exponential fashion.
    
*   ##### Advanced[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#advanced "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Pattern Length[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#pattern-length "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the length of the beat in number of frames. 10 means, for example, that the 10 frames around the given example beat are compared to other audio amplitude of remaining parts in order to find the other occurences of the beat.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

#### Animating to Musik - Part 1[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-automatic/#animating-to-musik---part-1 "Direct link to heading")

##### tip

[![Animating to Musik - Part 1](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/linear_link_news-eb2c52ef1375fc7b73e297c33fab7e1f.png)](http://www.sternfx.com/tutorials/105)

 for sternfx.com uses different audio and linking iExpressions to animate things automatically according to music.

# Autofocus on POI | Mamoworld Docs

Autofocus on POI
================

![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)

If you apply this iExpression to the Focus Distance of a camera, the focus automatically keeps the Point of Interest in focus.

*   ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/autofocus-poi/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    Animate this property if you want to temporarily shift the focus away from the point of interest. You can link this to a slider and apply a wiggle to the slider, for example, to simulate some "pumping" auto-focus.

# Auto Scale | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale/#)

Auto Scale
==========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/auto_scale_0-bf34836cf4ad5a2c255afa663fd7c6da.png)

With this expression you can move layers away from the camera (or towards it) while simultaneously scaling them, so that from the perspective of the camera they look unchanged.

This is useful, for example, when you have an image (like a matte painting) that consists of several layers representing different levels of depth - like foreground, middle ground and background elements.

With this expression you can easily distribute layers in 3d space to give your scene depth, as well as create parallax effects.

*   ##### Reference Frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale/#reference-frame "Direct link to heading")
    
    The frame at which the layers should look as if their position has not been shifted at all.
    
*   ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    How much the layer is moved away from the camera (use negative values to move towards the camera)
    
*   ##### Offset Multiplier[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale/#offset-multiplier "Direct link to heading")
    
    For a value of 20, each layer moves 20 times as far as determined by "Offset"; for 0 all layers are remain at their original position. If you give different layers a different "Offset", you can still animate them simultaneously by linking the "Offset Multiplier" of all of them to the same slider control.
    
*   ##### Camera[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale/#camera "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Use Camera[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale/#use-camera "Direct link to heading")
    
    Determines whether the iExpression should use the active camera, or the camera specified in the next parameter
    
    ##### Custom Cam Name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale/#custom-cam-name "Direct link to heading")
    
    Name of the camera to be used when 'Use Camera' is set to 'custom'.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Autofocus on Layers | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/autofocus-layers/#)

On this page

Autofocus on Layers
===================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/autofocus_on_layers_3_web-49a98db5098579381bc6b0f5b80632d4.png)

Automate the focus distance of your camera to keep a particular layer in focus - also transitions between layers are possible.

Just apply this expression to the "Focus Distance" property of your camera layer. Now the meaning of the "Focus Distance" value changed: 1 does not mean "focus distance 1px" anymore, but means "keep layer 1 in focus" instead.

### Transitions[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/autofocus-layers/#transitions "Direct link to heading")

To do a focus transition from layer 3 to layer 5 of your comp, simply add two keyframes - first with value 3 and second with value 5. If you add easing to your keyframes, the focus pull will ease accordingly, too! Of course, you can add as many keyframes as you want.

### Explicitly linking layers[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/autofocus-layers/#explicitly-linking-layers "Direct link to heading")

The only inconvenience of using layer indices for the focus distance is that you have the update the keyframes accordingly, if the index of your focus layers changes (if you add or delete other layers). To avoid this, you can connect layers explicitly by setting "Choose Layer by" to "explicit link". Now properties "Focus Layer 1" to "Focus Layer 30" appear, where you can connect layers that you want to focus on.

**Example:** Say you have in total five layer that you want to focus on. Then simply connect the first five "Focus Layer" properties of the iExpression to these five layers. Now keyframing the focus distance to 1 does not mean to focus on layer #1 of the comp anymore, but instead it means to focus the layer that you connected at the "Focus Layer 1" property of the iExpression - and this will not change, no matter how the actual index of the connected layer might change.

**Note - Missing Transition Parameters:** Older versions of this expressions had some transition parameters to control the timing from one layer to the next one. In the new version, the easing is directly taken from the easing of the keyframes. Therefore, the easing parameters are not needed anymore.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Transitions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/autofocus-layers/#transitions)
    
*   [Explicitly linking layers](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/autofocus-layers/#explicitly-linking-layers)

# Auto Scale Random | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#)

Auto Scale Random
=================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/auto_scale_random_0-c89ea4182359f70570bf3bf6b4c942d2.png)

With this expression, you can move layers randomly away from the camera (or towards it) while simultaneously scaling them, so that from the perspective of the camera they look unchanged.

This is useful, for example, if you have many text layers and want them to look as if they are all on the same plane in 3D space with the same size. If you turn the camera, however, you notice that some of them are very close and small, while others are far away and much bigger.

If you want to control for each layer exactly how much it is shifted, you can use the "Auto Scale" expression instead.

*   ##### Reference Frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#reference-frame "Direct link to heading")
    
    The frame at which the layers should look as if their position has not been shifted at all.
    
*   ##### Min Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#min-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    The minimum amount that a layer can be moved away from the camera (use a negative value to allow moving towards the camera)
    
*   ##### Max Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#max-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    The maximum amount that a layer may be moved away from the camera (use a negative value to only allow moving towards the camera)
    
*   ##### Offset Multiplier[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#offset-multiplier "Direct link to heading")
    
    For a value of 20, each layer moves 20 times as far as determined by "Min Offset" and "Max Offset"; for 0, all layers remmainat their original position. Animate this value to move the layers further away or back to their original position.
    
*   ##### Camera[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#camera "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Use Camera[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#use-camera "Direct link to heading")
    
    Determines whether the iExpression should use the active camera or the camera given in the next parameter
    
    ##### Custom Cam Name[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#custom-cam-name "Direct link to heading")
    
    Name of the camera that should be used when 'Use Camera' is set to 'custom'.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/camera/auto-scale-random/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    Change this number arbitrarily to get another random shift in the layers. For each number you enter, a different random value is generated.

# Beat Detector from Marker | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-marker/#)

On this page

Beat Detector from Marker
=========================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/beat_detector_from_marker_0-5a61085122805e4e958f148cf9559f81.png)

##### tip

[BeatEdit for After Effects](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-after-effects/)
 has a very different, powerful and in particular very easy to use approach to animate properties in sync with music.

Beat Detection iExpressions can be applied to properties which represent the audio amplitude of some music (i.e. properties which you created with the function 'convert audio into keyframes' of the keyframe assistent). They can detect the beats in the music such that you can animate various properties according to the music. The beat detectors just recognize the beat and don't do any animation, yet. You can animate properties using other iExpressions (like the 'Change on Beat 1D') which are then linked to the beat detector. This setup allows to animate several properties according to the same beat. To use the 'Beat Detector from Marker', you need to play the music and set marker according to it as if you would tab along with the rhythm. The iExpression then generates one beat for each marker. If you reapeat a regular pattern with the markers at least three times, the iExpression automatically continues this pattern if you enable the option 'continue automatically'.

*   ##### continue automatically[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-marker/#continue-automatically "Direct link to heading")
    
    enables the iExpression to detect the regular pattern in the marker placement and to continue it automatically.
    
*   ##### Fade In/Out[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-marker/#fade-inout "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Fade In Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-marker/#fade-in-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the number of frames over which the beat is faded in. If this is set to 0, the iExpression abruptly jumps on each beat from 0 to 100. If it is set to 10, the value starts already 10 frames earlier to slowly increase from 0 to 100.
    
    ##### Fade Out Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-marker/#fade-out-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the number of frames over which the beat is faded out. If this is set to 0, the iExpression abruptly jumps after each beat from 100 to 0. If it is set to 10, the value slowley decreases over 10 frames from 100 to 0.
    
    ##### Fade Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-detector-marker/#fade-type "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines whether the beat signal fades in or out in a linear or exponential fashion.

# Change on Beat | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/change-beat/#)

On this page

Change on Beat
==============

##### tip

[BeatEdit for After Effects](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-after-effects/)
 has a very different, powerful and in particular very easy to use approach to animate properties in sync with music.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/change_on_beat_2d-cb640fef56b9b7359fe69b3e3fd36a3d.png)

With this iExpression you can make a property react on a beat signal (slider) which you created using one of the beat detector iExpressions.

When there is no beat the property has its normal value given by the keyframes as if no iExpression would be applied. When there is a beat, the value changes to what you specify in "Value on Beat" (or the "Value on Beat" is added to the current value, if "Mode" is set to "add to original value").

There exists different _Change on Beat_ iExpressions, for 1D, 2D and 3D properties, texts and colors, respectively.

# Beat Mixer | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#)

Beat Mixer
==========

##### tip

[BeatEdit for After Effects](https://aescripts.com/beatedit-for-after-effects/)
 has a very different, powerful and in particular very easy to use approach to animate properties in sync with music.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/beat_mixer_0-8a42ed918844c0c6175fc707f8cd25f7.png)

The Beat Mixer creates a new beat as a combination of several other beats. This is useful if you want to animate a property such that it reacts on several different beats in the same way. For this, you first merge all the beats into a new beat using the 'Beat Mixer' and then you animate the property using this combined beat as you would use any other beat. If you want the property to react on more than one beat, but react on each of them differently, you can use one of the 'Beat Switch' iExpressions instead

*   ##### Smooth[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#smooth "Direct link to heading")
    
    controls the behaviour if several different beats overlap in time. Without 'Smooth' the result changes apruptly from the value of the one beat to the value of the other, whereas with the 'Smooth' option there is a smooth transition between the different beats.
    
*   ##### Beat 1[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#beat-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### link to Beat 1[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#link-to-beat-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    must be linked to the first of the beats which should be combined.
    
    ##### Level Beat 1 (%)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#level-beat-1- "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to modify the amplitude of beat 1 such that the reaction on it gets stronger or weaker.
    
*   ##### Beat 2[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#beat-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### link to Beat 2[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#link-to-beat-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    must be linked to the second of the beats which should be combined.
    
    ##### Level Beat 2 (%)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#level-beat-2- "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to modify the amplitude of beat 2 such that the reaction on it gets stronger or weaker.
    
*   ##### Beat 3[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#beat-3 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### link to Beat 3[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#link-to-beat-3 "Direct link to heading")
    
    must be linked to the third of the beats which should be combined (or be set to 0 if you only want to combine two beats).
    
    ##### Level Beat 3 (%)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#level-beat-3- "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to modify the amplitude of beat 3 such that the reaction on it gets stronger or weaker.
    
*   ##### Beat 4[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#beat-4 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### link to Beat 4[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#link-to-beat-4 "Direct link to heading")
    
    must be linked to the fourth of the beats which should be combined (or be set to 0 if you want to combine less than four beats).
    
    ##### Level Beat 4 (%)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/audio/beat-mixer/#level-beat-4- "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to modify the amplitude of beat 4 such that the reaction on it gets stronger or weaker.

# Distribute 2D Grid | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#)

Distribute 2D Grid
==================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_2d_grid-0b94f38f60c9b7679b6fcfb6c6619640.png)

With this iExpression you can distribute layers on a 2D grid. Just specify the first layer and all other layers that you apply the iExpression to, will be placed next to this layer in the given distance. You can still move layers away from their grid position using their keyframe values or the wiggle parameters.

*   ##### First Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#first-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### First Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#first-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first layer. This layer is the upper left corner of the grid and its position determines the position of the grid.
    
    ##### Position[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#position "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the position of the first layer in the grid.
    
*   ##### Distance[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#distance "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the distance between neighouring layers in the grid.
    
*   ##### Grid Size[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#grid-size "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes how many layers should be placed in one row and one column.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to move each layer away from its position in the grid using keyframes. In order to be exactly placed at its position in the grid, a layer then must have exactly the same keyframed position as the first layer of the grid. If the position is different, the difference is used as an offset to the grid position.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its position in the grid with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its grid position. To create a little less regular but still not moving grid, set the frequency to 0 and the amplitude to some value greater 0.
    
*   ##### Layer Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#layer-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-grid/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to delete the first rows or columns of the grid and insert them again behind the last rows or columns. A value of (3,2), for example, moves the first three columns behind the last one and the two topmost rows below the bottommost one.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Align to Composition | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-composition/#)

Align to Composition
====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/align_to_composition_0-916526135a724be84d457eead1c6e083.png)

This iExpression allows to align layers relative to the composition, i.e. they can be placed centered or at the borders of the composition. This placement is dynamic, i.e. if you change the composition size the placement adjusts automatically.

*   ##### Align X[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-composition/#align-x "Direct link to heading")
    
    aligns the layer horizontally. If 'none' is choosen, the keyframed x-value remains unchanged.
    
*   ##### Align Y[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-composition/#align-y "Direct link to heading")
    
    aligns the layer vertically. If 'none' is choosen, the keyframed y-value remains unchanged.
    
*   ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-composition/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to move the layer away from its aligned position. This is useful, for example, if you want to place the layer at the border of the composition but keep a certain distance to the border.
    
*   ##### Center around[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-composition/#center-around "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines which point of the layer is aligned relatively to the composition. When 'anchor point' is choosen the position of the layer is changed such that the anchor point is placed where specified (i.e. centered or at the border of the composition). If you choose 'layer boundaries', the boundaries of the layer are aligned to the boundaries of the composition, i.e. when aligning at the top, the top border of the layer is placed at the top border of the composition. The option 'layer boundaries' is only working for footage-based layers and not for other layers like text layers, as for those expression cannot determine the boundaries.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Attach to Cylinder 3D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-cylinder-3d/#)

Attach to Cylinder 3D
=====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/attach_to_cylinder_3d-39575bfaa3313cff8e37b9e3d2b3398b.png)

This iExpression changes the meaning of the x, y, and z value of the property such that you can easily animate it along a cylinder surface. Technically speaking the iExpression turns your coordinate system into a cylindrical coordinate system. Simply speaking, you can animate the property via keyframes as usual, but the values have a new meaning: Applied to the position of a layer, the x and y position now do not move the layer left/right and up/down anymore; instead they move the layer along the surface of the cylinder. The radius of the cylinder is given in the z component. In order to orient the layers correctly on the cylinder surface, apply the "Look At Line 3D" iExpression to their orientation and choose as first point of the line the center of the sphere and as second point an arbitrary point exactly above or below it (e.g. the center with a value of 100 added to its y value). If you want to move or rotate the cylinder, its best to set the center to (0,0,0), then create a new NULL object, make it 3D and parent the layers on the cylinder surface to this NULL. During the parenting keep the alt or option key pressed. Then the position of the NULL becomes the center of the cylinder and you can move or rotate it using the transform controls of the NULL.

*   ##### Center[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-cylinder-3d/#center "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the center of the cylinder along whose surface the property is moved.
    
*   ##### Scale Radius (%)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-cylinder-3d/#scale-radius- "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to scale the circle around which the cylinder wraps. If the x and y values are not identical you obtain squeezed circles i.e. ellipses.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Distribute 2D Circle and Spiral | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#)

Distribute 2D Circle and Spiral
===============================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_2d_circle_and_spiral-18867dc2f41903383aeb2a4504335fb6.png)

With this iExpression you can place layers in a circle. Variants like ellipses, spirals or arcs of circles are also possible. In order to orient the layers correctly on the circle, apply the "Look At Point 2D" iExpression to their rotation and choose as "Look At" point the center of the circle. If you want to move or rotate the circle, its best to set the center to (0,0), then create a new NULL object and parent the layers on the circle surface to this NULL. During the parenting, keep the alt or option key pressed. Then the position of the NULL becomes the center of the circle and you can move or rotate it using the transform controls of the NULL.

*   ##### Center[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#center "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the center of the circle (or of the ellipse, spiral,...)
    
*   ##### First Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#first-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first layer that should be placed in the circle.
    
    ##### Angle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#angle "Direct link to heading")
    
    this angle gives the direction where (relative to the center) the first layer is placed. 0 degree means above the center (12 o clock), 90 degree to the right of if (3 o clock), 180 degree below it (6 o clock) etc.
    
    ##### Radius[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#radius "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the distance that the first layer has to the center. For circles, this value is identical in both dimensions, for ellipsis you can choose different values in x and y direction.
    
*   ##### Last Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#last-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#layer-name-or-index-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the last layer that should be placed in the circle.
    
    ##### Angle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#angle-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    this angle gives the direction where (relative to the center) the last layer is placed. 0 degree means above the center (12 o clock), 90 degree to the right of if (3 o clock), 180 degree below it (6 o clock) etc.
    
    ##### use Radius of First Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#use-radius-of-first-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option the last layer uses the radius of the first layer (and ignored its own radius). Use this to create circles and ellipses; for spirals the radius of the first and last layer must be different.
    
    ##### Radius[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#radius-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the distance that the first layer has to the center. For circles, this value is identical in both dimensions, for ellipsis you can choose different values in x and y direction. For spirals this parameter should differ from the respective parameter for the first layer.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    adds the keyframed value to the position on the circle. Hence, the layer is only placed exactly on the circle when the keyframed position is (0,0). Other values cause a corresponding offset and allow to animate the position relative to its placement on the circle.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its position in the circle with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its position on the cirle. To create a little less regular but still not moving circle, set the frequency to 0 and the amplitude to some value greater 0.
    
    ##### Radial Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#radial-wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layers stay exactly on the circle while they wiggle. In other words, only the angle is wiggled, but not the radius.
    
*   ##### Layer Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#layer-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-circle-and-spiral/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    with an Offset of one, the first layer is removed from the beginning of the arc and added again at the end behind the last layer. For an offset of 2 this happens for the first two layers etc.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Attach to Circle 2D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-circle-2d/#)

Attach to Circle 2D
===================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/attach_to_circle_2d-353838b53e8123c304f3f0cacb962243.png)

This iExpression changes the meaning of the x and y value such that you can easily animate the property along circles, ellipses or spirals. Technically speaking, the iExpression turns your coordinates into polar coordinates. Simply speaking, after you applied the iExpression, you can animate the property via keyframes as usual but they have a different meaning: Applied to the position of a layer, the x and y position now to not specify anymore how much the layer moves left/right and up/down; instead the x value moves the layer along circles around the center point and the y value gives the radius of this circle, i.e. moves the layer towards and away from the center. If you animate the position of a layer using 2 keyframes, e.g. from \[0,50\] to \[360,50\], the layer moves one complete round (0 to 360 degrees) on a circle of radius 50. To move on spirals, also animate the y component over time. Moving e.g. from \[0,0\] to \[720,100\] yields a movement along a spiral from the center of the circle outward up to a radius of 100, where in total two full rotations are performed (720=2\*360). In order to orient the layers correctly on the circle, apply the "Look At Point 2D" iExpression to their rotation and choose as "Look At" point the center of the circle. If you want to move or rotate the circle, its best to set the center to (0,0), then create a new NULL object and parent the layers on the circle surface to this NULL. During the parenting, keep the alt or option key pressed. Then the position of the NULL becomes the center of the circle and you can move or rotate it using the transform controls of the NULL.

*   ##### Center[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-circle-2d/#center "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the center of the circle (or of the ellipse) around which the property moves.
    
*   ##### Scale (%)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-circle-2d/#scale- "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to scale the circle. If the x and y values are not identical you obtain squeezed circles, i.e. ellipses.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Align Layers in Row/Column | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-layers-rowcolumn/#)

On this page

Align Layers in Row/Column
==========================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-11-12_10.58.50_0-0e4d07bf525c393f82c799e001085557.png)

The _Align Layers in Row_ and _Align layers in column_ expressions allow you to create dynamic layouts. The placement of the layers is calculated automatically and when the content changes (say you replace some text or change the scale of some layer), the placement of all layers changes instantly such that they stay aligned perfectly.

Just connect in the expression parameters all layers that should be aligned and then apply the expression to the position of all connected layers. Now you can move the first and last layer freely and all other layers distribute in-between automatically.

### Animate In/Out[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-layers-rowcolumn/#animate-inout "Direct link to heading")

By default, the expressions keep the layers aligned on all frames. If you want to move layers individually, to animate them into or out of their aligned position, you should

1.  choose the option to align only on a single frame (choose some frame where everything should be aligned).
2.  add a keyframe to the position of all aligned layers at this frame
3.  Now you can add keyframes at other points in time to move the layers relative to their aligned position

Note that the keyframes you create work relative to the aligned position now. So If you change the content, still all layers update their position and all keyframed animations are shifted accordingly.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-layers-rowcolumn/#example "Direct link to heading")

![After Effects layers aligned in column with expression. When content changes, layout updates instantly](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/iexpressionsalignlayerscolumn_preview_gif-d5bfdb6f036b08f1c6b753df3d7c3123.gif)

Here we aligned some text layers and a shape layer (the line) in a column. If you move the first or last layer (or change the content of any of the layers) everything updates such that the layers always preserve and equal distance in Y direction and stay centered in X direction. You can still animate the layers in and out, since the expression is set to align only on a single frame.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Animate In/Out](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-layers-rowcolumn/#animate-inout)
    
*   [Example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/align-layers-rowcolumn/#example)

# Attach to Sphere 3D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-sphere-3d/#)

Attach to Sphere 3D
===================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/attach_to_shpere_3d-8a05a753383320f29c16bec9f1dff901.png)

This iExpression changes the meaning of the x, y, and z value of the property such that you can easily animate it along a sphere surface. Technically speaking the iExpression turns your coordinate system into a spherical coordinate system. Simply speaking, you can animate the property via keyframes as usual but the values have a new meaning: Applied to the position of a layer, the x and y position now do not move the layer left/right and up/down anymore; instead they move the layer along the surface of the sphere. The radius of the sphere is given in the z component. In order to orient the layers correctly on the sphere surface, apply the "Look At Point 3D" iExpression to their orientation and choose as "Look At" point the center of the sphere. If you want to move or rotate the sphere, its best to set the center to (0,0,0), then create a new NULL object, make it 3D and parent the layers on the sphere surface to this NULL. During the parenting, keep the alt or option key pressed. Then the position of the NULL becomes the center of the sphere and you can move or rotate it using the transform controls of the NULL.

*   ##### Center[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-sphere-3d/#center "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the center of the sphere along whose surface the property is moved.
    
*   ##### Scale Radius (%)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/attach-sphere-3d/#scale-radius- "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to scale the sphere. If the x y and z values are not identical you obtain squeezed spheres (i.e. elliptical ones).
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Wiggle | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#)

On this page

Wiggle
======

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/basic_wiggle_0-51f612b08887c1474592db91f200c851.png)

**Wiggle and Jiggle layers in After Effects easily**  
iExpressions includes a big collection of different wiggle expressions for After Effects. In addition to the normal _Basic Wiggle_ it contains a seamlessly looping wiggle, one that snaps to a grid and also wiggles where you can control the wiggle settings for up/down and left/right movement separately, for example.

### Fade Wiggle In and Out[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#fade-wiggle-in-and-out "Direct link to heading")

To fade any of the wiggle expressions in and out, you can link the wiggle amplitude to a slider and then keyframe it. This also allows you to change the amount of wiggling over time.

### Variants[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#variants "Direct link to heading")

![Basic Wiggle Expression for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/basic_wiggle-6de2b29ccc7d7e6b11cd52976e587bef.gif)

#### Basic Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#basic-wiggle "Direct link to heading")

This is the simple wiggle that you also get when writing this expressions code manually:

    wiggle(frequency, amplitude)

The _Frequency_ parameter says how fast it should wiggle (wiggles per second) and the _Amplitude_ parameter says how much it should wiggle (maximum amount in pixels).

![Directional Wiggle Expression for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/directional_wiggle-f9b82d6b1e46b0f9fa88f7e8e4521659.gif)

#### Directional Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#directional-wiggle "Direct link to heading")

The _Directional Wiggle_ has an _Angle_ parameter and only wiggles in the direction specified by that angle. Of course, you can also link this angle to a slider to change the wiggle direction over time.

![Looping Wiggle Expression for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/looping_wiggle-568301a599d9209a78366b2652144ee1.gif)

#### Looping Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#looping-wiggle "Direct link to heading")

The _Looping Wiggle_ behaves exactly as the _Basic Wiggle_, except that at the last frame of the layer it ends exactly at the position where it started. This is very helpful if you want to create a wiggle animation that loops seamlessly.

![One Side Wiggle Expression for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/one_side_wiggle-f21b8a0d1edd2c663f5ec7fe25409b6f.gif)

#### One Side Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#one-side-wiggle "Direct link to heading")

In this example, we let the right icon wiggle upwards when the left icon bounces on the ground. These kinds of secondary animations let impacts appear to be more realistic and intense. Since we use the _One Side Wiggle_, it only wiggles upwards (note that it never goes below the ground line).

iExpressions contains _One Side Wiggle_ expressions for 1D, 2D and 3D properties. The 1D variant can be applied to rotation (say wiggle the hand of a speedometer) or opacity, for example.

![Separate Wiggle Expression for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/separate_wiggle-7449fa81473ddace859496a67ad3022a.gif)

#### Separate Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#separate-wiggle "Direct link to heading")

The _Separate Wiggle_ is the same as the _Basic Wiggle_, except that wiggle frequency and amplitude can be controlled for X and Y direction independently. In this example, we wiggle 100px left/right but only 50px up/down. Also the frequency is set to 10 wiggles per second for left/right but only 3 wiggles per second for up/down. As a result, the layer wiggles much more and faster to the side than up and down.

The _Separate Wiggle_ comes in a 2D and a 3D variant. In the 3D variant you can control frequency and amplitude not only in X and Y but also in Z direction.

![Snapping Wiggle Expression for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/snapping_wiggle-0af1d4601a74ac554109161a4c75be43.gif)

#### Snapping Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#snapping-wiggle "Direct link to heading")

The _Snapping Wiggle_ is like a combination of a _Basic Wiggle_ and a [Snap 2 Grid iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/movement-modifier/snap-grid-1d2d3d)
, so it first moves the layer in a random direction and then snaps the position to a grid. The expression also offers a smoothness parameter.

[Learn more](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/snapping-wiggle-1d2d3d)

![Temporal Wiggle Expression for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/temporal_wiggle-ed03c64ffb163585b7e17b47368bf879.gif)

#### Temporal Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#temporal-wiggle "Direct link to heading")

In this example, we use the _Temporal Wiggle_ to wiggle the layer along its keyframed motion path. So without the wiggle it would move along the line with constant speed and with the wiggle it moves back and forth along the path in a random way instead.

In general, the _Temporal Wiggle_ can be applied to any keyframed property. Instead of using the keyframe value at the current time, the expression then moves back and forth in time randomly and picks the keyframe values of these other points in time.

![Wave Wiggle Expression for After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/wave_wiggle-b9e95da0e7bbdbb96c6dd31a5c4f8d33.gif)

#### Wave Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#wave-wiggle "Direct link to heading")

The _Wave Wiggle_ is very similar to the _Basic Wiggle_, but creates a more regular, curved movement. So if the _Basic Wiggle_ is too chaotic or has too abrupt turns for you, try the _Wave Wiggle_ instead.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Fade Wiggle In and Out](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#fade-wiggle-in-and-out)
    
*   [Variants](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle/#variants)

# Snapping Wiggle 1D/2D/3D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/snapping-wiggle-1d2d3d/#)

On this page

Snapping Wiggle 1D/2D/3D
========================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-10-14_14.22.24-c564c686ed0ac60d3ecfa73767391858.png)

These expressions are like a combination of a [Wiggle expression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle)
 and a Snap to Grid iExpression.

So instead of a continuous movement, the wiggle happens in steps of a customable step size. The 2D and 3D variants are useful to move layers along a grid, the 1D variant can be used, for example, to rotate elements.

[See More Wiggles](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle)

### Examples[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/snapping-wiggle-1d2d3d/#examples "Direct link to heading")

![After Effects Snapping Wiggle expression - Rotation Example Sci-Fi HUD](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/snapping_wiggle_example_rotation-41d665d36332feb1b5407cd8216f6a70.gif)

Here we applied the Snapping Wiggle 1D expression to the rotation of the different layers. In contrast to the simple wiggle, the rotation is not continuous, but consists of fixed degree steps. Due to the smoothness parameter, the rotation does not jump from one step to the next one, but still travels smoothly.

![After Effects Snapping Wiggle expression - Rotation Example Sci-Fi HUD](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/snapping_wiggle_example_position-72a37bc3da2ff4e8f5aa8e485e4dc251.gif)

Here we applied the Snapping Wiggle 1D to a lot of semi transparent solid layers. In contrast to the Basic Wiggle, with the Snapping Wiggle the layers stay on the grid. Due to the smoothness parameter, they do not jump abruptly from one grid cell to the next cell, but travel there smoothly. If your grid has a different size in X and Y direction, use the Snapping Wiggle 2D, if the grid size is the same in all dimensions, you can also use the 1D variant.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Examples](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/snapping-wiggle-1d2d3d/#examples)

# Android Spring | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/android-spring/#)

On this page

Android Spring
==============

Design spring animations in After Effects in the same way as [Android's Spring Animation](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/dynamicanimation/animation/SpringAnimation)
 and use them for implementation in Android, iOS and React!

[Tutorial](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/spring-animations-adobe-after-effects)

[More details](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/ux-spring-animations-adobe-after-effects)

This expression interpolates between keyframes in the same way as [Android's Spring Animation](https://developer.android.com/reference/androidx/dynamicanimation/animation/SpringAnimation)
 animations and has exactly the same parameters. It allows you to not only easily create a spring animation in After Effects, it also calculates all the parameters you need to recreate the exact same behavior with Android's Spring Physics, UIKit’s UISpringTimingParameters, or SwiftUI’s interpolatingSpring and react-spring. So you can design the animations with the Android spring parameters and then easily implement them across all platforms so they always feel the same, whether your user is in a web app, or using an Android or Apple phone.

### Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/android-spring/#parameters "Direct link to heading")

Android's Spring Animation describes spring animations with damping ratio and stiffness. Instead of dealing with a ton of different keyframe types, easings and durations, you describe each move with just two numbers! Android has four default values for both damping and stiffness, and the following chart demonstrates what all combinations of these values look like.

![Android Spring Animation - Stiffness and DampingRatio](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/andriod_spring_grid_optimized-1608a39725efcfd61e62a4ede0fb8f33.gif)

### Creating Animations[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/android-spring/#creating-animations "Direct link to heading")

Android Spring creates time-less animations, i.e. you don't specify the duration of movements or motion path curves. Instead you just specify a target position and how the object is attracted by this target position (as if your object is pulled towards that target by an invisible spring). When you apply this expression, your keyframes turn into target positions. So if you have two keyframes, for example, immediately after the first keyframe, the value is attracted by the second keyframes and moves towards it based on the spring physics parameters you set in the expression. Only the value of the keyframes is considered, the easing you set in Ae is ignored and replaced by the spring simulation. You can have as many keyframes as you want, to move the target position over time and can apply the expression to arbitrary 1D, 2D and 3D properties (and to texts for parameter conversion - see next section).

### Converting Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/android-spring/#converting-parameters "Direct link to heading")

Since the expression has exactly the same spring parameters as Android spring, it is easy to implement the animation in Android. But if you need to implement the same animation in iOS or react, you need the corresponding parameters for those spring systems, first. To obtain those, just apply the expression to the source text of a text layer. The expression will then calculate the parameters for UIKit, SwiftUI and react-spring from the Android parameters and then output all of them on the text layer.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Parameters](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/android-spring/#parameters)
    
*   [Creating Animations](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/android-spring/#creating-animations)
    
*   [Converting Parameters](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/android-spring/#converting-parameters)

# Tutorial: Spring Animations in Adobe After Effects | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/spring-animations-adobe-after-effects/#)

On this page

Tutorial: Spring Animations in Adobe After Effects
==================================================

#### Can't wait to see new tutorials?[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/spring-animations-adobe-after-effects/#cant-wait-to-see-new-tutorials "Direct link to heading")

[Subscribe](https://mamoworld.com/subscribe)
 In our newsletter we share all new tutorials already before they are published. Subscribe to watch new tutorials before everybody else!

### Chapters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/spring-animations-adobe-after-effects/#chapters "Direct link to heading")

1.  What are spring animations?
2.  Import Designs from Figma, Sketch and Xd to After Effects
3.  Creating spring animations in Ae
4.  Android spring parameters
5.  Spring animations on scale
6.  Implementing springs in Android, iOS and React
7.  Converting spring parameters between Android, iOS and React
8.  Overlapping actions
9.  Initial velocity parameter

Today we show you something about a little motion design niche: Designing user interface animations for mobile apps and web apps.

Learn how to first import your user interface designs from Xd, Figma or Sketch into Adobe After Effects, then create animations there using the spring tools in [iExpressions](https://aescripts.com/iexpressions/)
. It’s not only easy to create spring animations in After Effects, they can also be easily implemented in Google’s Android, Apple’s UIKit (or SwiftUI), and Facebook’s React.

One major potential hassle is that the parameters for spring animations are different for each platform - Google, Apple and Facebook all cook their soup a little differently. Fortunately, the After Effects extension iExpressions comes to the rescue. It allows you to not only easily create a spring animation in After Effects, it also calculates all the parameters you need to recreate the exact same behavior with Android Spring Physics, UIKit’s UISpringTimingParameters, or SwiftUI’s interpolatingSpring and react-spring. So even though all these spring animation systems are very different, once you’ve designed the animation in After Effects, you’ll have an easy recipe to precisely implement the same animation across all major platforms.

[Learn More](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/ux-spring-animations-adobe-after-effects)

# React Spring 8 | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/react-spring-8/#)

On this page

React Spring 8
==============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-10-18_11.07.33-e21e9fc717982e40e7faec5f0085ae23.png)

Design animations in Adobe After Effects for [react-spring](https://www.react-spring.io/)
!

Don't know what react-spring is? [Learn more](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/prototyping-react-spring-animations-adobe-after-effects)

This expression interpolates between keyframes in the same way as [react-spring](https://www.react-spring.io/)
 (version 8) and has exactly the same parameters. So if the animations you create should be implemented in react-spring, simply use this expression instead of normal keyframes and when implementing the animation in react-spring, simply copy and paste the parameters from the expression to get exactly the same animation in your web app.

react-spring creates time-less animations, i.e. you don't specify the duration of movements or motion path curves. Instead you just specify a target position and how strongly the object is attracted by this target position. When you apply this expression, your keyframes turn into target positions. So if you have two keyframes, for example, immediately after the first keyframe, the value is attracted by the second keyframes and moves towards it based on the spring physics parameters you set in the expression. Only the value of the keyframes is considered, the easing you set in Ae is ignored and replaced by the spring simulation. You can have as many keyframes as you want, to move the target position over time and can apply the expression to arbitrary 1D, 2D and 3D properties.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/react-spring-8/#example "Direct link to heading")

![how react-spring animations work](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/spring-systems-explanation_0-78aa03d0c7f17822f7080db49ae8d511.gif)

Here we visualized how a spring system is working. The green box is the target position given by your keyframes and the orange circle moves as calculated by the react-spring expression. The upper one moves with default spring settings and in the lower one we changed the mass from 1 to 4 such that the animation has more inertia and overshooting.

# UIKit Spring | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/uikit-spring/#)

On this page

UIKit Spring
============

Design spring animations in After Effects in the same way as Apple's spring animation systems in UIKit/SwiftUI and use them for implementation in Android, iOS and React!

[Tutorial](https://mamoworld.com/tutorials/spring-animations-adobe-after-effects)

[More details](https://mamoworld.com/blog/ux-spring-animations-adobe-after-effects)

This expression interpolates between keyframes in the same way as the spring systems of Apple's UIKit or SwiftUI and has exactly the same parameters. It allows you to not only easily create a spring animation in After Effects, it also calculates all the parameters you need to recreate the exact same behavior with Android Spring Physics, UIKit’s UISpringTimingParameters, or SwiftUI’s interpolatingSpring and react-spring. So you can design the animations with the UIKit spring parameters and then easily implement them across all platforms so they always feel the same, whether your user is in a web app, or using an Android or Apple phone.

### Creating Animations[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/uikit-spring/#creating-animations "Direct link to heading")

UIKit Spring creates time-less animations, i.e. you don't specify the duration of movements or motion path curves. Instead you just specify a target position and how the object is attracted by this target position (as if your object is pulled towards that target by an invisible spring). When you apply this expression, your keyframes turn into target positions. So if you have two keyframes, for example, immediately after the first keyframe, the value is attracted by the second keyframes and moves towards it based on the spring physics parameters you set in the expression. Only the value of the keyframes is considered, the easing you set in Ae is ignored and replaced by the spring simulation. You can have as many keyframes as you want, to move the target position over time and can apply the expression to arbitrary 1D, 2D and 3D properties (and to texts for parameter conversion - see next section).

### Converting Parameters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/spring-systems/uikit-spring/#converting-parameters "Direct link to heading")

Since the expression has exactly the same spring parameters as springs in UIKit and SwiftUI spng, it is easy to implement the animation in Apple devices. But if you need to implement the same animation in Android or react, you need the corresponding parameters for those spring systems, first. To obtain those, just apply the expression to the source text of a text layer. The expression will then calculate the parameters for Android and react-spring from the UIKit/SwiftUI parameters and then output all of them on the text layer.
