# Softlink Puppet Pin To Layers | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#)

Softlink Puppet Pin To Layers
=============================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/soft_link_puppet_pin_ui_0-a9badea72cb11dad149639c553dac404.png)

This expression links a pin from the puppet tool to several layers, such that all these layers influence the movement of the pin. This is like connecting the pin with rubber bands to all these layers. With a weight you can control the influence of each individual layer (the less weight, the softer is the rubber band for this layer). This is like a simple form of weight painting used for skinning characters in 3d applications. You can also set a time delay for each individual layer to create the impression of an even more elastic connection to this layer.

*   ##### Layer 1 (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-1-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    Name or index of the first layer to which the puppet pin should be connected.
    
*   ##### Layer 1 Weight[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-1-weight "Direct link to heading")
    
    The higher this value, the more influence this layer has on the pin.
    
*   ##### Layer 1 Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-1-options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Time Delay (frames)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#time-delay-frames "Direct link to heading")
    
    for 10, for example, the pin reacts with a delay of 10 frames to each movement of the layer.
    
    ##### Layer Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-space-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    Offset, in case the pin should not be linked to the anchor point of the layer but to another point in the layer space.
    
    ##### Comp Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#comp-space-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    Moves the link in composition space. For \[100,0\], for example, the pin is always 100 pixels right of the anchor point of the layer. In contrast to the Layer Space Offset, the Comp Space Offset is not affected by rotation or scale of the layer that the pin is linked to.
    
*   ##### Layer 2 (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-2-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    Name or index of the first layer to which the puppet pin should be connected.
    
*   ##### Layer 2 Weight[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-2-weight "Direct link to heading")
    
    The higher this value, the more influence this layer has on the pin.
    
*   ##### Layer 2 Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-2-options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Time Delay (frames)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#time-delay-frames-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    for 10, for example, the pin reacts with a delay of 10 frames to each movement of the layer.
    
    ##### Layer Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-space-offset-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Offset, in case the pin should not be linked to the anchor point of the layer but to another point in the layer space.
    
    ##### Comp Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#comp-space-offset-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Moves the link in composition space. For \[100,0\], for example, the pin is always 100 pixels right of the anchor point of the layer. In contrast to the Layer Space Offset, the Comp Space Offset is not affected by rotation or scale of the layer that the pin is linked to.
    
*   ##### Layer 3 (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-3-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    Name or index of the first layer to which the puppet pin should be connected.
    
*   ##### Layer 3 Weight[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-3-weight "Direct link to heading")
    
    The higher this value, the more influence this layer has on the pin.
    
*   ##### Layer 3 Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-3-options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Time Delay (frames)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#time-delay-frames-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    for 10, for example, the pin reacts with a delay of 10 frames to each movement of the layer.
    
    ##### Layer Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-space-offset-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Offset, in case the pin should not be linked to the anchor point of the layer but to another point in the layer space.
    
    ##### Comp Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#comp-space-offset-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Moves the link in composition space. For \[100,0\], for example, the pin is always 100 pixels right of the anchor point of the layer. In contrast to the Layer Space Offset, the Comp Space Offset is not affected by rotation or scale of the layer that the pin is linked to.
    
*   ##### Layer 4 (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-4-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    Name or index of the first layer to which the puppet pin should be connected.
    
*   ##### Layer 4 Weight[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-4-weight "Direct link to heading")
    
    The higher this value, the more influence this layer has on the pin.
    
*   ##### Layer 4 Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-4-options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Time Delay (frames)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#time-delay-frames-3 "Direct link to heading")
    
    for 10, for example, the pin reacts with a delay of 10 frames to each movement of the layer.
    
    ##### Layer Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-space-offset-3 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Offset, in case the pin should not be linked to the anchor point of the layer but to another point in the layer space.
    
    ##### Comp Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#comp-space-offset-3 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Moves the link in composition space. For \[100,0\], for example, the pin is always 100 pixels right of the anchor point of the layer. In contrast to the Layer Space Offset, the Comp Space Offset is not affected by rotation or scale of the layer that the pin is linked to.
    
*   ##### Layer 5 (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-5-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    Name or index of the first layer to which the puppet pin should be connected.
    
*   ##### Layer 5 Weight[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-5-weight "Direct link to heading")
    
    The higher this value, the more influence this layer has on the pin.
    
*   ##### Layer 5 Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-5-options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Time Delay (frames)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#time-delay-frames-4 "Direct link to heading")
    
    for 10, for example, the pin reacts with a delay of 10 frames to each movement of the layer.
    
    ##### Layer Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#layer-space-offset-4 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Offset, in case the pin should not be linked to the anchor point of the layer but to another point in the layer space.
    
    ##### Comp Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#comp-space-offset-4 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Moves the link in composition space. For \[100,0\], for example, the pin is always 100 pixels right of the anchor point of the layer. In contrast to the Layer Space Offset, the Comp Space Offset is not affected by rotation or scale of the layer that the pin is linked to.
    
*   ##### Pin Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/softlink-puppet-pin-layers/#pin-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    For \[100,0\], for example, the pin is always 100 pixels right of the normal position.

# Pendulum | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/pendulum/#)

Pendulum
========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/pendulum_0-e8d8797c05a3694789343acd1037bb86.png)

With this iExpression you can rotate a layer such that it oscilates like a pendulum. The point around which the pendulum rotates is the anchor point of the layer. On 2D layers the iExpression can be applied to the rotation, on 3D layers it can be applied to the x rotation, y rotation or z rotation.

*   ##### Start Angle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/pendulum/#start-angle "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines, in which angle (in degrees) the pendulum starts.
    
*   ##### Speed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/pendulum/#speed "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the speed with which the pendulum starts oscillating.
    
*   ##### Decay[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/pendulum/#decay "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines how fast the movement decays.
    
*   ##### Simulation Start[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/pendulum/#simulation-start "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Start at[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/pendulum/#start-at "Direct link to heading")
    
    controls at which point in time the simulation starte (at the inpoint of the layer, an a manually chosen keyframe or at a manually chosen frame)
    
    ##### (Key) Frame No.[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/pendulum/#key-frame-no "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the frame or the keyframe (depending on the choice of "Start at" option) at which the pendulum simulation starts. When "Start at" has the value 'inpoint of layer' this option is ignored.

# Rotating Wheel Curvaceous Motion | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#)

Rotating Wheel Curvaceous Motion
================================

![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)

This iExpression computes the rotation of a wheel based on the movement of the layer. This can be used to rotate the wheels or a car or any other kinds of wheels that roll on the ground. If the wheel only moves along a straight line and does not change its size over time, you can use the faster iExpression 'Rotating Wheel Linear Motion' instead.

*   ##### Diameter[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#diameter "Direct link to heading")
    
    the diameter of the wheel (in number of pixels)
    
*   ##### Use Movement From[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#use-movement-from "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Use[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#use "Direct link to heading")
    
    Choose if the rotation should be computed according to the movement of the layer itself or according to the movement of another point.
    
    ##### Link to Other Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#link-to-other-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    link here the other point, to whose movment the according rotation should be computed.
    
*   ##### Ground[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#ground "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### use ground layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#use-ground-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    if the ground itself moves relative to the composition, use this option.
    
    ##### Ground Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#ground-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the ground layer relative to which the wheel should rotate.
    
*   ##### Advanced[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#advanced "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Specify Diameter in[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#specify-diameter-in "Direct link to heading")
    
    Specifies whether die diameter is given in composition or in layer space. Example: Say the wheel is a solid of size 100 pixels and is scaled to 200%. With the option 'layer coordinates' you should set the diameter to 100 but with the option 'comp coordinates' you should set it to 200, since from the comp's perspective, the solid has a size of 200 pixels.
    
    ##### Flip Direction[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#flip-direction "Direct link to heading")
    
    Activate this option if the wheel rotates in the wrong direction. This may happen when the ground is above the wheel instead of below it.
    
    ##### Skip Frames[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-curvaceous-motion/#skip-frames "Direct link to heading")
    
    Increasing this value makes the computation faster but less accurate. For a value of 5, the iExpression calculates the motion path only on every 6th frame and assumes that the wheel travels from one such point to the next in a straight line.

# Rotating Wheel Linear Motion | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#)

Rotating Wheel Linear Motion
============================

![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)

This iExpression computes the rotation of a wheel based on the movement of the layer. This can be used to rotate the wheels or a car or any other kinds of wheels that roll on the ground. This iExpression has the following limitations: (a) the wheel may only move along a straight line (for example horizontally, but any other direction is also possible). On this line it may move both forwards and backwards. (b) the size of the wheel may not change over time. If you have more complex animations where either (a) or (b) is not satisfied, use the iExpression "Rotating Wheel Curvaceous Motion" that can handle these cases but computes a bit slower.

*   ##### Diameter[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#diameter "Direct link to heading")
    
    the diameter of the wheel (in number of pixels)
    
*   ##### Use Movement From[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#use-movement-from "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Use[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#use "Direct link to heading")
    
    Choose if the rotation should be computed according to the movement of the layer itself or according to the movement of another point.
    
    ##### Link to Other Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#link-to-other-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    link here the other point, to whose movment the according rotation should be computed.
    
*   ##### Ground[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#ground "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### use ground layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#use-ground-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    if the ground itself moves relative to the composition, use this option.
    
    ##### Ground Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#ground-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the ground layer relative to which the wheel should rotate.
    
*   ##### Advanced[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#advanced "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Specify Diameter in[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#specify-diameter-in "Direct link to heading")
    
    Specifies whether die diameter is given in composition or in layer space. Example: Say the wheel is a solid of size 100 pixels and is scaled to 200%. With the option 'layer coordinates' you should set the diameter to 100 but with the option 'comp coordinates' you should set it to 200, since from the comp's perspective, the solid has a size of 200 pixels.
    
    ##### Flip Direction[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/rotating-wheel-linear-motion/#flip-direction "Direct link to heading")
    
    Activate this option if the wheel rotates in the wrong direction. This may happen when the ground is above the wheel instead of below it.

# Roller Coaster Master | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#)

Roller Coaster Master
=====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/roller_coaster_master_0-8147afe62aa42451d19d00ce9663b4b2.png)

This iExpressions lets a 2D or layer move like a roller coaster. To use it, proceed in the following steps. 1) Draw the roller coaster track as a mask path 2) Copy the mask path and paste it to the position property of the layer. Now the layer moves with a constant speed along the roller coaster track. Instead of using a mask path, you can also keyframe this motion by hand (e.g. when the desired path is 3D). You only need to make sure to make the keyframes "rove accross time" such that the layer moves with constant speed before the iExpression is applied. 3) Apply the iExpression to the position of the layer and adjust gravity, start position, and start speed to get the desider movement 4) Adjust max speed and friction to fine-tune the movement. You can also link those properties to sliders to animate them over time. This allows, for example to manually slow down the roller coaster at specific points in time. Now you have a single Layer that moves like a rollercoaster. If you want to animate several layers such that they behave like different wagons of the same roller coaster, you better do not apply the "Roller Coaster Master" to each of them, but better proceed as follows. I) Animate a dummy layer (e.g. a NULL layer or solid) as described in steps 1-4 II) Duplicate the dummy layer and set the "Output" property of the iExpression to "Master Data" in the first and to "Track Data" in the second copy. IV) Apply the "Roller Coaster Wagon" iExpression to position and rotation of each layer that should become a rollercoaster wagon. Link the parameters "Link To Master Data" and "Link To Track Data" to the positions of the respective layer from step II. Use the "Position Offset" parameter to position the different wagons. V) If a wagon is rotated upside down, set its (keyframe value of) rotation from 0 to 180.

*   ##### Start Position (Frame)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#start-position-frame "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines, at which position of the track the roller coaster starts. A value of 20 means, for example, that the roller coaster starts at the position where it has been at frame 20 before the iExpression was applied.
    
*   ##### Start Speed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#start-speed "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the initial speed of the roller coaster
    
*   ##### Physics[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#physics "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Gravity[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#gravity "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines how fast the roller coaster accelerates when it moves downhill.
    
    ##### Friction[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#friction "Direct link to heading")
    
    Determines how well the wheels of the roller coaster are oiled. The higher the fricion, the more the roller coaster deaccelerates. You can also link this property to a slider. When you increase the friction at some point in time this is as if the pilot of the roller coaster would start breaking.
    
    ##### Max Speed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#max-speed "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the maximum speed the roller coaster can have. Whenever the roller coaster tends to gets faster, it is automatically deaccelerated. You can also animate this value over time by linking it to a slider. By this you can, for example, stop the roller coaster at some point in time by keyframing the max speed at this point to 0.
    
*   ##### Output[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#output "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Output[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#output-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    leave this value at 'preview' to see the movement. The other two alternatives are needed only in combination with the "Roller Coaster Wagon" iExpression.
    
    ##### Quality[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-master/#quality "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines the quality of the physics simulation. The higher this value is the slower is the computation but the more realistic is the movement.

# Move In & Out 3D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#)

Move In & Out 3D
================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/move_in_out_3d-2d80d84b09a4d8acb668caa51d9f56cc.png)

With this iExpression you can move a 3D layer in and out of screen using a uniformly accelerated movement in any direction. At first sight the motion might seem similar to using 'easy ease' keyframes, but due to the uniform deceleration when moving in and uniform acceleration when moving out, the movement is slightly different and more natural. The direction is given via two angles (like in a spherical coordinate system).

*   ##### Acceleration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#acceleration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines how fast the object decelerates when moving in and accelerates when moving out.
    
*   ##### Angle In (horizontal,vertical)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#angle-in-horizontalvertical "Direct link to heading")
    
    ![angle_move_in_out_3d.png](<Base64-Image-Removed>)
    
    is the angle (i.e. the direction) from which the object is moving into the frame. The first component denotes the horizontal angle, i.e. the angle on the ground plane (like shown on a compass). It the camera has its default orientation, 0 degree means moving in from z direction, -90 degree from left and 90 degree from right. 180 degree means moving in from the viewers point of view. The second component denotes the vertical angle. 0 degree means a purely horizontal movement, 45 degree moving in diagonally from above and -45 degree from diagonally below. 90 degree means moving in vertically from the top and -90 degree from the bottom.
    
*   ##### Angle Out (horizontal, vertical)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#angle-out-horizontal-vertical "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the angle (i.e. the direction) in which the object is moving out of the frame. The first component denotes the horizontal angle, i.e. the angle on the ground plane (like shown on a compass). It the camera has its default orientation, 0 degree means moving out in z direction, -90 degree to the left and 90 degree to the right. 180 degree means moving towards the viewers point of view. The second component denotes the vertical angle. 0 degree means a purely horizontal movement, 45 degree moving out diagonally upwards and -45 degree diagonally downwards. 90 degree means moving straight up and -90 degree straight down.
    
*   ##### Move In Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#move-in-time "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Move In At[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#move-in-at "Direct link to heading")
    
    controls whether a movement into the frame should be enabled and if so at which point in time this movement should be finished (at the first keyframe, an a manually chosen keyframe, at a manually chosen frame, or no movement into the frame at all).
    
    ##### Move In (Key) Frame No.[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#move-in-key-frame-no "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the frame or the keyframe (depending on the choice of 'Move In At' option) at which the movement into the frame should be finished. If 'Move In At' has any other value than 'keyframe no.' or 'frame no.' this value is ignored.
    
*   ##### Move Out Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#move-out-time "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Move Out At[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#move-out-at "Direct link to heading")
    
    controls whether a movement out of the frame should be enabled and if so at which point in time this movement should start (at the first keyframe, an a manually chosen keyframe, at a manually chosen frame, or no movement out of the frame at all).
    
    ##### Move Out (Key) Frame No.[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-3d/#move-out-key-frame-no "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the frame or the keyframe (depending on the choice of 'Move Out At' option) at which the movement out of the frame starts. If 'Move Out At' has any other value than 'keyframe no.' or 'frame no.' this value is ignored.

    ##### Layer Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/link-puppet-pin-layer/#layer-space-offset "Direct link to heading")

    ##### Comp Space Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/link-puppet-pin-layer/#comp-space-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    Moves the link in composition space. For \[100,0\], for example, the pin is always 100 pixels right of the anchor point of the layer. In contrast to the Layer Space Offset, the Comp Space Offset is not affected by rotation or scale of the layer that the pin is linked to.

# Move In & Out 2D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#)

Move In & Out 2D
================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/move_in_out_2d-d7ec775cbd4e3d5e1fed9e12175e40a1.png)

With this iExpression you can move a 2D layer in and out of screen using a uniformly accelerated movement in any direction. At first sight the motion might seem similar to using 'easy ease' keyframes, but due to the uniform deceleration when moving in and uniform acceleration when moving out, the movement is slightly different and more natural.

*   ##### Acceleration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#acceleration "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines how fast the object decelerates when moving in and accelerates when moving out.
    
*   ##### Angle In[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#angle-in "Direct link to heading")
    
    ![angle_move_in_out_2d.gif](<Base64-Image-Removed>)
    
    is the Angle (i.e. the direction) from which the object is moving into the frame. 0 = from the top, 90 = from right, 180 = from the bottom, 270 = from left
    
*   ##### Angle Out[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#angle-out "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the Angle (i.e. the direction) in which the object is moving out of the frame. 0 = move up, 90 = move to the right, 180 = move down, 270 = move to the left
    
*   ##### Move In Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#move-in-time "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Move In At[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#move-in-at "Direct link to heading")
    
    controls whether a movement into the frame should be enabled and if so at which point in time this movement should be finished (at the first keyframe, an a manually chosen keyframe, at a manually chosen frame, or no movement into the frame at all).
    
    ##### Move In (Key) Frame No.[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#move-in-key-frame-no "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the frame or the keyframe (depending on the choice of 'Move In At' option) at which the movement into the frame should be finished. If 'Move In At' has any other value than 'keyframe no.' or 'frame no.' this value is ignored.
    
*   ##### Move Out Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#move-out-time "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Move Out At[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#move-out-at "Direct link to heading")
    
    controls whether a movement out of the frame should be enabled and if so at which point in time this movement should start (at the first keyframe, an a manually chosen keyframe, at a manually chosen frame, or no movement out of the frame at all).
    
    ##### Move Out (Key) Frame No.[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/move-out-2d/#move-out-key-frame-no "Direct link to heading")

# Arrowhead | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/arrowhead/#)

Arrowhead
=========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-10-18_20.19.41-69d4b236ad0a065088d914dd0f900579.png)

Creates an arrow head that can be attached to any shape. The arrow head is a separate path such that it can be styles independently of the shape it is attached to. The appearance of the arrowhead can be controlled in full detail using the iExpressions parameters.

**Usage** To attach an arrowhead to a path

1.  duplicate the path (or the entire shape layer)
2.  connect the original path in the expression parameter "Attach to Path"
3.  apply the arrow head to the duplicate path - it will stick now to the path it is connected to

![Fill between arrow head and line](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-10-25_09.34.23-181cffd9907b61b631537956c5bcd2f0.png)

**Tip** Make sure to keep both the path for your line and the path for the arrowhead on the same shape layer, to make sure the arrow head stays attached to the line when you move it. Best place the arrow head path above the line path, such that you can place a fill between the two path. That way the arrow head is filled, but the line is not.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# IK Arm (with 2 or 3 Segments) | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/ik-arm-2-or-3-segments/#)

On this page

IK Arm (with 2 or 3 Segments)
=============================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-10-22_13.28.47-6aa3cf38d4458c2a18145818e18d43b4.png)

This iExpression allows to animate an entire arm by just specifying the point where the hand should be - the expression calculates the rotation of all arm segments accordingly (using Inverse Kinematics, IK). You tell where the arm should move - the expression knows how.

This expression comes in two variants:

*   **2 Segment Arm** A normal arm with upper arm and lower arm
*   **3 Segment Arm** A arm with upper arm , "middle arm" and lower arm - you can use this, for example, to animate a finger (which in contrast to an arm consists of three segments) or other objects, like the plant in our example.

**Usage**  
The arm must consist of the following layers:

*   upper arm (with anchor point at shoulder)
*   lower arm (with anchor point at elbow)
*   hand (with anchor point at wrist)
*   The 3 segment variant needs a middle element in addition - see tutorial for details.

Each arm layer must be parented to the respective next arm segment. So for the 2 segment variant the hand must be parented to the lower arm and the lower arm to the upper arm. In addition there must be a (null-) layer that represents the goal where the arm should point.

When the arm is set up like this, all you need to do is to connect the respective layers into the iExpression and apply it to the rotation of all arm layers and the hand (but not the hand goal).

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/ik-arm-2-or-3-segments/#example "Direct link to heading")

![Inverse Kinematic Arm with 3 Segments - After Effects expression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/iexpressionsikarm3segments_preview-f1a821a19eab80df5f093f7e0c8b475a.gif)

This "arm" consists of 3 segments - as you can see, the stem of the plant consists of three parts or on other words has "2 elbows" instead of one. Therefore, we used the 3 segment variant of the expression.

Also note that the expression makes sure that the head always points in the same direction no matter how the arm it is attached to rotates. You can still keyframe the head, if you want to rotate it, but don't have to deal with unwanted rotations when you move the arm.

![Robot Character IK Rig After Effect expression for inverse kinematics](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/iexpressionsikarm2segments_preview-6aa89e459a50dd9c2f4e8ff5f3b21f0a.gif)

In this example we applied the "IK Arm with 2 Segments" expression to each of the arms and legs separately.

As a result, the character can now be animated by moving the body and any of the 4 goal layers for hands and feet. To let these kinds of characters walk, also take a look at the [Walk Cycle iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/walk-cycle)
.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/ik-arm-2-or-3-segments/#example)

# Roller Coaster Wagon | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-wagon/#)

Roller Coaster Wagon
====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/roller_coaster_wagon_0-290532397de1c508cfce881e9bc12870.png)

This iExpression is used in combination with the "Roller Coaster Master" to animate a series of layers as if they where different wagons of a roller coaster. For more details see the description of "Roller Coaster Master".

*   ##### Link To Master Data[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-wagon/#link-to-master-data "Direct link to heading")

    link here a copy of the Roller Coaster Master, whose output is set to "Track Data".
    
*   ##### Position Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/roller-coaster-wagon/#position-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to build a roller coaster out of several layers (where each layer behaves like one wagon of the rollercoaster). The wagon in the middle should have a position offset of 0 and the wagons before and after it should have appropriate positive or negative offsets to place them before or after the middle wagon.

# Elastic Connection | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/elastic-connection/#)

Elastic Connection
==================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-11-11_11.41.24-19a55d941451f81dbe42b3e9e2880d3f.png)

With this iExpression you can connect a property to another one in an elastic way. This is like a linking expression, but the property follows the linked property not directly, but in a delayed, elastic way.

If you connect the position of a layer to the position of another layer, they behave as if they where connected by an elastic strap. However, you can also apply it to other properties - for example to make the scale of one layer follow the scale of another one in an elastic way.

Note that the expression only acts in one direction: The property that you apply the expression to follows the linked one but not vice versa.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Cog Wheels | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/cog-wheels/#)

Cog Wheels
==========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/cog_wheels_0-05cbca9c1175c857ba3532f12580ae09.png)

Computes the rotation of a cog wheel (gear wheel) from the rotation of the other cog wheel that it is connected to. The iExpression can also be used for wheels or rollers that are rotating agains each other. In this case, specify the diameter or circumfence of the wheels instead of the number of theeth.

*   ##### Link To Other Wheel Rotation[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/cog-wheels/#link-to-other-wheel-rotation "Direct link to heading")
    
    Link here the rotation of the cog wheel that is driving this cog wheel.
    
*   ##### This Wheel Number of Teeth[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/cog-wheels/#this-wheel-number-of-teeth "Direct link to heading")
    
    How many teeth this cog wheel has (instead of the number of teeth you can also enter the diameter or circumfence of the two wheels)
    
*   ##### Other Wheel Number of Teeth[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/cog-wheels/#other-wheel-number-of-teeth "Direct link to heading")
    
    How many teeth this other cog wheel has which is driving this cog wheel (instead of the number of teeth you can also enter the diameter or circumfence of the two wheels).
    
*   ##### Flip Direction[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/cog-wheels/#flip-direction "Direct link to heading")
    
    Makes the wheel move in the opposite direction. This is only required in special cases, like for internal gears that have their theeth inside and not at the outside of the wheel.

# Elastic | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/elastic/#)

On this page

Elastic
=======

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-10-17_10.13.04-b3c0bbdefb47eedee0ce925dfbe9dfc4.png)

Makes the shape deform and react to movements of the layer in an elastic way. Shape parts are deformed more, the further they are away from the anchor point of the layer.

Note that the expression only reacts on movements of the layer (or its parents) but not on changes of the path it self.

**Tip:** If your have multiple path, vary the parameters (like delay and wobble frequency) slightly to get a rich and dynamic result.

### Examples[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/elastic/#examples "Direct link to heading")

![motion trails shape layer After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/iexpressiontrail_preview-61e1124dc60257f82ec0b8017bf494a9.gif)

In this example we wiggled position and rotation of the text and then converted it to shapes such that we could apply the Elastic iExpression, which adds some deformation to the text. Finally we applied the Trail expression to a few paths on a new shape layer to create the white speed lines.

![Throw 2D, Trail and Elastic expressions have been used for this animation](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/throw-aa6dde1338a1e90dfb607e876373294b.gif)

For this animation we combined

*   the [Throw 2D with Walls](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/throw-2d3d)
     iExpression for the motion
*   the [Trail iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/trail)
     for the speed lines and
*   the _Elastic iExpression_ to deform the logo

Instead of linking the trail expression directly to the logo, we linked it to a null layer with the same position as the logo, but no rotation. That way the trails follow the position, but not the rotation. We also applied the [Basic Wiggle iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle)
 to this null to wiggle the speed lines a bit.

![wobble elastic shape path in After Effects with expression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/comparison_plant_elastic_wobble-ed0a517797aa0748bbe955ca5aaf68f6.gif)

In this example each of the two branches is one shape layer with the elastic expression applied to its path. The anchor point of the layers is at the bottom of the branch, such that the deformation originates from that point.

**Variation:** In the wobble variant, we varied the wobble frequency a bit (3 vs 3.5) such that the branches don't swing in sync. A bit more subtle bit still visible is that in the variant without wobble the delay is slightly different for each of the two branches (0.2s vs. 0.25s), such that the darker, bigger branch overshoots a bit more and longer. These little variations are not obvious but add a lot of life and realism.

**Easing:** Wobble works best, it your layer stops abruptly, hence, we used linear keyframes for the variant with wobble. Without wobble, the overshooting looks much better with eased keyframes. Therefore, in the other variants, we applied an ease in, such that the motion stops softly.

![reset group transforms for shape layers when using the expression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/reset_group_transforms-e3b68156ea748bb78667e2e74ee5db98.png)

If the path is nested inside groups, make sure that all their transforms are at default values. You can animate the layer by animating the layer transforms or using parenting. If you have several shapes that should move in different ways, place them on separate layers. All of this is necessary, since the expression measures the movement of the shape based on the movement of the layer's anchor point. So if you animate using transforms of individual groups, this movement is ignored by the expression. Also, the deformation is calculated such that it originates from the anchor point, and this only works properly, if the individual group transforms are reset to zero. If you notice that the shape does not stick to the anchor point during deformation, make sure to reset the group transforms.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Examples](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/elastic/#examples)

# Linear Link 3D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#)

Linear Link 3D
==============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/linear_link_3d-f58f013ce69bcf022ce11d045f0feb0e.png)

This iExpression links a 3D property to a slider (or some other 1D property - like audio keyframes). You specify which value the 3D property should have when the slider has its minimum value and which value it should have when the slider has its maximum value. For all other slider values the iExpression chooses automatically an appropriate value (using linear interpolation).

*   ##### Link to Slider[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#link-to-slider "Direct link to heading")
    
    Don't put a number here. Use the link button to link this to a slider control, an audio amplitude or some other 1D property.
    
*   ##### Slider Min[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#slider-min "Direct link to heading")
    
    the smallest value of the slider control (or of the audio level or whatever other property you link to)
    
*   ##### Slider Max[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#slider-max "Direct link to heading")
    
    the largest value of the slider control (or of the audio level or whatever other property you link to)
    
*   ##### Value for Min[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#value-for-min "Direct link to heading")
    
    the value that the property should have when the linked slider has its minimal value
    
*   ##### Value for Max[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#value-for-max "Direct link to heading")
    
    the value that the property should have when the linked slider has its maximal value
    
*   ##### Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Clip Borders[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#clip-borders "Direct link to heading")
    
    If this is enabled, the property will never have values smaller than 'Value for Min' or larger than 'Value for Max', even if the slider that the property is linked to has values smaller than 'Slider Min' or larger than 'Slider Max'.
    
    ##### Link x[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#link-x "Direct link to heading")
    
    Without this option the X value is not linked but taken from the keyframes instead
    
    ##### Link y[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#link-y "Direct link to heading")
    
    Without this option the Y value is not linked but taken from the keyframes instead
    
    ##### Link z[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-3d/#link-z "Direct link to heading")
    
    Without this option the Z value is not linked but taken from the keyframes instead

# Link to Point on Shape | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/link-point-shape/#)

Link to Point on Shape
======================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-10-22_16.40.48-628e6324569b4b43d66f021f56afdb36.png)

This expression links the position (and optionally rotation) of a layer to an arbitrary point on a path or also to its Bezier handles. Works even if the path is nested into groups with non-default transforms!

**Rotation** To orient the layer along the path, simply apply the expression to both position and rotation of the layer. You can still keyframe the rotation on top of the rotation generated by the expression.

**Animate Along Path** To animate any layer along a path in After Effects, you need to

1.  make sure _mode_ is set to _percentage_
2.  link the _Attach Point_ parameter to a slider (by clicking on the chain link icon right before it)
3.  Keyframe the linked slider from 0 to 100 to move from the beginning to the end of the path.

Limited Time Parent
===================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-11-13_11.41.27-db41cfcbdf3fc0cf988d0b312137fa54.png)

This iExpression is an alternative to parenting layers. There are two major differences:

1.  You can parent individual properties (say only the position but not the scale or rotation).
2.  The parenting can be active for a limited amount of time and not necessarily for the entire duration of the composition.

# Rotation Link | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/rotation-link/#)

Rotation Link
=============

![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)

This iExpression links an arbitrary property to the rotation of a layer or any other value representing an angle, like an Angle Control. You can keyframe your property as usual specify a first and a last frame and then the keyframes between the first and the last frame are played in a loop for each full rotation. This is similar to a Linear Link Time Remap, but has a cyclic behaviour such for each full rotation, the same animation is played again.

*   ##### Link to Rotation[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/rotation-link/#link-to-rotation "Direct link to heading")
    
    Don't put a number here. Use the link button to link this to the rotation of a layer or any other Angle Control.
    
*   ##### Start Frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/rotation-link/#start-frame "Direct link to heading")
    
    the number of the first frame of the region that should be played at each rotation
    
*   ##### End Frame[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/rotation-link/#end-frame "Direct link to heading")
    
    the number of the first frame behind the region that should be played.
    
*   ##### Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/rotation-link/#options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Loop length[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/rotation-link/#loop-length "Direct link to heading")
    
    After how many degrees of rotation the animation starts again at the beginning. 360 degrees means that after one full rotation, the animation starts again at the beginning.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Linear Link 1D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-1d/#)

On this page

Linear Link 1D
==============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/linear_link_1d-35602c4e03625bbd70f80e1915d55437.png)

This iExpression links a 1D property to a slider (or some other 1D property - like audio keyframes). You specify which value the 1D property should have when the slider has its minimum value and which value it should have when the slider has its maximum value. For all other slider values the iExpression chooses automatically an appropriate value (using linear interpolation).

*   ##### Link to Slider[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-1d/#link-to-slider "Direct link to heading")
    
    Don't put a number here. Use the link button to link this to a slider control, an audio amplitude or some other 1D property.
    
*   ##### Slider Min[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-1d/#slider-min "Direct link to heading")
    
    the smallest value of the slider control (or of the audio level or whatever other property you link to)
    
*   ##### Slider Max[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-1d/#slider-max "Direct link to heading")
    
    the largest value of the slider control (or of the audio level or whatever other property you link to)
    
*   ##### Value for Min[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-1d/#value-for-min "Direct link to heading")
    
    the value that the property should have when the linked slider has its minimal value
    
*   ##### Value for Max[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-1d/#value-for-max "Direct link to heading")
    
    the value that the property should have when the linked slider has its maximal value
    
*   ##### Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-1d/#options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Clip Borders[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-1d/#clip-borders "Direct link to heading")
    
    If this is enabled, the property will never have values smaller than 'Value for Min' or larger than 'Value for Max', even if the slider that the property is linked to has values smaller than 'Slider Min' or larger than 'Slider Max'.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Linear Link Time Remap | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-time-remap/#)

On this page

Linear Link Time Remap
======================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-12-02_10.03.24-6559afeb789676e5d0a074e47b579513.png)

This iExpression links arbitrary keyframed animations to a slider.

### Usage[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-time-remap/#usage "Direct link to heading")

1.  keyframe an animation as usual
2.  Set the iExpression parameters _Frame for Min_ and _Frame for Max_ to the frames where your keyframed animation starts and ends, respectively.
3.  Optional: If the slider should have a different range than 0-100, set it in the iExpressions parameters _Slider Min_ and _Slider Max_
4.  Create a slider (Effect->Expressions Controls->Slider Control) and connect it to the iExpression
5.  Apply the expression to all properties you keyframed in step 1

Now the keyframed animation is linked to the slider, i.e. when you move the slider from 0 to 100, the animation plays back (and if you move the slider from 100 back to 0 it plays backwards). This is like time-remapping the keyframes, but instead of time-remapping an entire video clip, it only time-remaps the keyframes of the properties that the expression is applied to.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-time-remap/#example "Direct link to heading")

![Link Keyframes to Sliders in After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/iexpressionslinearlinktimeremapfatthin_preview-d3be28030a46b8510f6fd8592c388b8c.gif)

This character is created with a single shape layer.

First we keyframed the face details to transition from a happy face to a sad face and linked those properties to a happiness slider. Then we used keyframes on the remaining shape paths properties to transition from a slim, bearded character to a fat one and linked those keyframes to a weight slider. Finally, we keyframed the transform properties (scale and position) of the shape groups to shrink the character and linked this animation to a size slider.

### Tip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-time-remap/#tip "Direct link to heading")

Note that each property can only be controlled by one slider. If you want to transform a shape based on multiple sliders, you can nest the shape inside multiple groups. Since each group has its own transform controls, nesting into multiple groups allows you to to link each group transform to a different slider. Similarly, if you want to move a layer based on two sliders, you can parent the slider to a null. Then keyframe the movement for the first slider on the layer itself and the one for the second slider on the parent layer.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Usage](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-time-remap/#usage)
    
*   [Example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-time-remap/#example)
    
*   [Tip](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/linear-link-time-remap/#tip)

# Trail | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/trail/#)

On this page

Trail
=====

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-10-15_14.17.43-1fe864159da0608efb8005ddab1aa8f9.png)

Creates a line that follows the movement of another layer. To create multiple different lines, play with the parameters in the "Randomize" section and apply the expression to multiple path properties at the same time.

### Examples[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/trail/#examples "Direct link to heading")

![motion trails shape layer After Effects](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/iexpressiontrail_preview-61e1124dc60257f82ec0b8017bf494a9.gif)

In this example we wiggled position and rotation of the text and then converted it to shapes such that we could apply the Elastic iExpression, which adds some deformation to the text. Finally we applied the Trail expression to a few paths on a new shape layer to create the white speed lines.

![Throw 2D, Trail and Elastic expressions have been used for this animation](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/throw-aa6dde1338a1e90dfb607e876373294b.gif)

For this animation we combined

*   the [Throw 2D with Walls](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/throw-2d3d)
     iExpression for the motion
*   the _Trail iExpression_ for the speed lines and
*   the [Elastic iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/elastic)
     to deform the logo

Instead of linking the trail expression directly to the logo, we linked it to a null layer with the same position as the logo, but no rotation. That way the trails follow the position, but not the rotation. We also applied the [Basic Wiggle iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle)
 to this null to wiggle the speed lines a bit.

![Trails with After Effects shape expression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/trails_snap_to_grid-106407ff5f135238b3d072041ee97d78.gif)

In this example, we first applied the [Snap 2 Grid iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/movement-modifier/snap-grid-1d2d3d)
 to the position of the circle to create the zig zag motion path. Then we created a new shape layer and applied the Trail expression to its path to create the lines that follow the motion.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Examples](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/trail/#examples)

# Average Link | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/average-link/#)

Average Link
============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-11-11_11.42.56-c08b7922ce82e46e1719ede49ab795f4.png)

This iExpression links a 1D, 2D or 3D property to the average of two other properties. Average means that the property is always in the middle of the two linked properties. This is, for example, useful to average two trackpoints to get a more precise and robust tracking result. You can also compute weighted averages where one of the two points has a stronger influence than the other by using the 'balance' parameter.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Throw 2D/3D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/throw-2d3d/#)

On this page

Throw 2D/3D
===========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/throw_2d-38ad1cacf1e92db1f801a2f3a7fe9200.png)

With these iExpressions you can literally throw a layer such that it flies through the air and realistically bounces off the ground and optionally also walls.

Using this iExpression is very easy: Just keyframe the movement of the layer and after the last keyframe the simulation starts automatically. Hence, the throw direction and speed depends on the movement at the last keyframe. To get the desired movement you only need to adjust the height of the ground from which the layer bounces. You can also adjust the elasticity with which the layer bounces of the ground and the gravity with which the layer is attracted by the ground. In the variants of this expression with walls, you also need to specify the location of those walls and how elastic it bounces at those walls.

In total there are four _Throw_ iExpressions: A variant with walls and without for both 2D and 3D layers, respectively.

### Example[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/throw-2d3d/#example "Direct link to heading")

![Throw 2D, Trail and Elastic expressions have been used for this animation](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/throw-aa6dde1338a1e90dfb607e876373294b.gif)

For this animation we combined

*   the _Throw 2D with Walls_ iExpression for the motion
*   the [Trail iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/trail)
     for the speed lines and
*   the [Elastic iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/elastic)
     to deform the logo

Instead of linking the trail expression directly to the logo, we linked it to a null layer with the same position as the logo, but no rotation. That way the trails follow the position, but not the rotation. We also applied the [Basic Wiggle iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/wiggles/wiggle)
 to this null to wiggle the speed lines a bit.

![Bouncing Text in After Effects with expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/iexpressionsthrow_preview-bb161ee292833a77fdaf3a2838ceb039.gif)

Here we used the _Throw 2D_ iExpression to drop the "Heavy" text on the ground and the _Throw 2D with Walls_ to throw the "Light" text. For the heavy text, we lowered the _Elastic Bounce_ parameter from the default of 50% to only 20% such that the object feels as if it has more weight.

We also converted the texts to shapes and applied the [Elastic Path](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/path/elastic)
 iExpression to deform the texts based on their movement.

For the entire animation we just needed three keyframes! One for the start location of the heavy text and two for throwing the light text. All other movement and deformation is entirely derived from those three keyframes by the expressions.

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

*   [Example](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/physics-simulations/throw-2d3d/#example)

# Distribute 2D Line | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#)

Distribute 2D Line
==================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_2d_line-a12b7e0fafaff74741ecce90feac2f36.png)

With this iExpression you can place layers in a straight line. For horizontal or vertical lines, you can also use the 'Align Layers in Row' or 'Align Layers in Column' iExpressions, if you don't need the advanced parameters like the wiggle.

*   ##### First Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#first-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first layer that should be placed on the line.
    
*   ##### Last Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#last-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the last layer that should be placed on the line.
    
*   ##### Starting Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#starting-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the starting point of the line on which the layers should be distributed.
    
*   ##### Ending Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#ending-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the ending point of the line on which the layers should be distributed.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    adds the keyframed value to the position on the line. Hence, all layers except the first and the last one are then only placed exactly on the line when their keyframed position is (0,0). Other values cause a corresponding offset and allow to animate the position relative to its placement on the line.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its position on the line with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its position on the line. To create a little less regular but still not moving line, set the frequency to 0 and the amplitude to some value greater 0.
    
    ##### Wiggle On Line[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#wiggle-on-line "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layers stay exactly on the line while they wiggle. In other words, it only wiggles in the direction of the line.
    
*   ##### Layer Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#layer-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-line/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    with an offset of one, the first layer is removed from the beginning of the line and added again at the end behind the last layer. For an offset of 2 this happens for the first two layers etc.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Distribute 3D Cylinder | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#)

Distribute 3D Cylinder
======================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_3d_cylinder-9e3577ecbdeffa056e893954e4f15498.png)

With this iExpression you can distribute 3D layers on the surface of a cylinder. The layers are placed on circles around the clyinder surface and you can specify how many layers each circle should consist of and what distance the different circles should have. You can still move layers away from their position on the cylinder using their keyframe values or the wiggle parameters. In order to orient the layers correctly on the cylinder surface, apply the "Look At Line 3D" iExpression to their orientation and choose as first point of the line the center of the sphere and as second point an arbitrary point exactly above or below it (e.g. the center with a value of 100 added to its y value). If you want to move or rotate the cylinder, its best to set the center to (0,0,0), then create a new NULL object, make it 3D and parent the layers on the cylinder surface to this NULL. During the parenting keep the alt or option key pressed. Then the position of the NULL becomes the center of the cylinder and you can move or rotate it using the transform controls of the NULL.

*   ##### First Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#first-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first layer that should be placed on the cylinder.
    
*   ##### Center (of First Row)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#center-of-first-row "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the center of the cylinder at the height of the first circle of layers. All subsequent circles are placed below the first one.
    
*   ##### Radius[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#radius "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the radius of the cylinder. If the x and y component are not idendical, the cylinder does not look like a circle from the top, but like an ellipse.
    
*   ##### Circles[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#circles "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Layers per Circle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#layers-per-circle "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes how many layers should be placed in one circle around the cylinder surface before the next circle (which is placed below) is started.
    
    ##### Distance of Cirles[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#distance-of-cirles "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes the distance between the different circles on which the layers are placed on the cylinder surface.
    
    ##### Angle (Shift per Cirles)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#angle-shift-per-cirles "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to palce the layers on the second circle not exactly below the layers in the first circle but shifted by the given angle.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to move each layer away from its position on the cylinder surface using keyframes. In order to be exactly placed at its position on the cylinder, a layer then must have the keyframed position (0,0,0). If the position is different, the difference is used as an offset to the position on the cylinder surface.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its position on the cylinder surface with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its position on the cylinder surface. To create a little less regular but still not moving placement of the layers, set the frequency to 0 and the amplitude to some value greater 0.
    
    ##### Wiggle Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#wiggle-type "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines in which way the layers are wiggled. 'in all directions' moves the layers arbitrarily in 3D space (also away from the clyinder surface), 'on surface left/right/up/down' moves the layers in arbitrary directions on the surface of the cylinder , and 'on surface left/right' moves them only along the circles around the cylinder surface on which they are placed.
    
*   ##### Layer Row Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#layer-row-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Row Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#row-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    with an offset of one, the first row of layers is removed from the top and added again at the bottom below the last row. For an offset of 2 this happens for the first two rows of layers etc.
    
    ##### Number of Rows[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-cylinder/#number-of-rows "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes how many rows of layers around the cylinder exist. A value of 3 means, for example, that the first row is moved below the third row if there is a row offset.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Counter Numbers | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#)

Counter Numbers
===============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2020-01-07_15.34.00-9d7a25c20d09c4db8db47af08b738154.png)

This iExpression creates a text that can be used as a counter to create, for example, a countdown. The iExpression has some similarities with the effect Numbers, but offers several advantages: 1) numbers larger than 30 000 can be created 2) the decimal marker symbol can be configured freely 3) an optional thousands separator symbol can group the number into blocks of three digits 4) you can add text before and after the actual number 5) it works directly with the source text property to text layers instead of an effect on a solid layer.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/counter_numbers-73a4fba60a343c4afafc49102cf9fce4.gif)

*   ##### Slider[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#slider "Direct link to heading")
    
    link this to a slider control which you can keyframe to control the number shown by the counter
    
*   ##### Multiply Counter Value With[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#multiply-counter-value-with "Direct link to heading")
    
    normally 1. If you enter, for example, 1000 the number shown by the counter is 1000 times as large as the value of your linked slider. This is useful if you need to create numbers that are larger than the value range of slider controls.
    
*   ##### Decimal Places[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#decimal-places "Direct link to heading")
    
    how many fractional digits of the number are shown
    
*   ##### Leading Zeros[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#leading-zeros "Direct link to heading")
    
    for 5, for example, the number is extended with zeros until it contains 5 number of places before the decimal separator, i.e. 312 would become 00312
    
*   ##### Separator Symbols[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#separator-symbols "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Decimal Mark Symbol[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#decimal-mark-symbol "Direct link to heading")
    
    symbol used to separate the whole part from the fractional part of a number (usually . or ,)
    
    ##### Thousands Separator Symbol[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#thousands-separator-symbol "Direct link to heading")
    
    For ease of reading, numbers with many digits may be divided into groups of three digits using a delimiter. Enter here the delimiter symbol (e.g. a space, a point or nothing at all)
    
*   ##### Surrounding Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#surrounding-text "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text Before[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#text-before "Direct link to heading")
    
    an optional text that is inserted before the counter itself
    
    ##### Text After[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-numbers/#text-after "Direct link to heading")
    
    an optional text that is inserted after the counter itself
