# Counter Date | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#)

Counter Date
============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/counter_date_0-a1fa5b31a7b6f440c1070d71a1fdbc40.png)

With this iExpression you can create a date string and animate it over time. The date can be formatted freely. Simply link "Link to Counter Value" to a slider control and keyframe it to values between 0 and 100. Then you specify which date correpsonds to the value 0 and which one to 100. The iExpression figures out the date for all other values accordingly.

![Counter Date expression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/counter_date-8d15e1029dc81ed9def3bec85b111b77.gif)

*   ##### Link to Counter Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#link-to-counter-value "Direct link to heading")
    
    link this to a slider control which you can keyframe to control the date shown by the counter
    
*   ##### Date for Value 0[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#date-for-value-0 "Direct link to heading")
    
    date that should be shown when the linked slider control has the value 0. Enter the date with month names as words,e.g. "6 January 1972" or "Nov 23 2012"
    
*   ##### Date for Value 100[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#date-for-value-100 "Direct link to heading")
    
    date that should be shown when the linked slider control has the value 100. Enter the date with month names as words,e.g. "6 January 1972" or "Nov 23 2012"
    
*   ##### Date Format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#date-format "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Date Format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#date-format-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines, how exactly the date should look like. When the date string is created, this text is taken and symbols %W, %D, %D and %Y are replaced by the day of the week, day of the month, month, and year, respectively. You can arrange the symbols freely (and also leave some out if you want, for example, no weekday). Inbetween the symbols you can add arbitrary other text.
    
    ##### Year Format (%Y)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#year-format-y "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the year is formatted
    
    ##### Month Format (%M)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#month-format-m "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the month is formatted
    
    ##### Day Format (%D)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#day-format-d "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the day of the month is formatted
    
    ##### Weekday Format (%W)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#weekday-format-w "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the weekday is formatted
    
    ##### Month Names[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#month-names "Direct link to heading")
    
    Here you can translate the name of each month into your language
    
    ##### Weekday Names[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#weekday-names "Direct link to heading")
    
    Here you can translate each day of the week into your language
    
*   ##### Surrounding Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#surrounding-text "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text Before[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#text-before "Direct link to heading")
    
    an optional text that is inserted before the date itself
    
    ##### Text After[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date/#text-after "Direct link to heading")
    
    an optional text that is inserted after the date itself

# Distribute 2D Random | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#)

Distribute 2D Random
====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_2d_random-8a9ffe28ae9156e34fa509d8c9888c68.png)

With this iExpression you can distribute 2D layers (or other 2D properties) randomly. You can still move layers away from their random position using their keyframe values or the wiggle parameters. You can influence the random distribution also with the help of some layer: The lighter the layer is at some position, the more likely it is that a random value is placed at this position.

*   ##### Minimum Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#minimum-value "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the minimum of the possible random values. Note that if you use the wiggle or keyframe offset options, the value could become smaller that this.
    
*   ##### Maximum Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#maximum-value "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the maximum of the possible random values. Note that if you use the wiggle or keyframe offset options, the value could become larger that this.
    
*   ##### Image based Probability[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#image-based-probability "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Use Image Based Probability[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#use-image-based-probability "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to determine based on the lightness of some layer the probabilies where the random positions appear. The more white the layer is at some position, the more likely it is that at this point a random value is placed.
    
    ##### Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the layer whose lightness is used to determine where to place the random values.
    
    ##### Precision[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#precision "Direct link to heading")
    
    When the layer consists mostly of very dark areas the computation might become slow. If you decrease the precision, the computation gets faster but it is possible that more random values occur in the dark (unlikely) areas.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    adds the keyframed value of the property to the random value. This allows you to move the property away from its keyframed value using keyframes.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its random position with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its random position when it wiggles.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    can be modified to get other random values.
    
    ##### Change Over Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-2d-random/#change-over-time "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to get a new random value for each frame.

# Distribute Text to Layers | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/distribute-text-layers/#)

Distribute Text to Layers
=========================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_text_to_layers_0-cc2eff5eeacc1f1fcf8865553172019e.png)

This iExpression offers a simple possibility to distribute a text to several layers. Each single layer can contain one or several letters, words or lines of the original text.

*   ##### First Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/distribute-text-layers/#first-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    the name or index of the first of the layers to which the text is distributed
    
*   ##### Source Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/distribute-text-layers/#source-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    the name or index of the text layer that contains the text which should be distributed (leave empty to use the text of the layer itself - this also makes the iExpression faster)
    
*   ##### Split Into[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/distribute-text-layers/#split-into "Direct link to heading")
    
    whether each layer contains one letter, one word, or one line of the text
    
*   ##### Advanced[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/distribute-text-layers/#advanced "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Layer Order[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/distribute-text-layers/#layer-order "Direct link to heading")
    
    in which order the letters are distributed to the layers
    
    ##### Letters/Words/Lines per Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/distribute-text-layers/#letterswordslines-per-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    How many letters/words/lines are on the same layer.
    
    ##### Identical Copies[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/distribute-text-layers/#identical-copies "Direct link to heading")
    
    for 3, always three successive layers obtain the same text

# Linear Wipe | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/linear-wipe/#)

Linear Wipe
===========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/linear_wipe_0-328f23820b25ce2a3bba36d0be548961.png)

Creates a transition from one text to another one by replacing the first text letter by letter with the second text. This iExpression is applied to the source text property of a text layer. Create for each text a keyframe and the iExpression computes automatically the transition between the keyframes. If some text should remain unchanged for some time, simply create two keyframes with the same text at the beginning and end of this region.

![Linear Wipe Text Transition](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/text_transition_linear_wipe-b87a48fff5493db9b884bd58cc81a35f.gif)

*   ##### Direction[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/linear-wipe/#direction "Direct link to heading")
    
    the order in which the letters are replaced.
    
*   ##### Align[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/linear-wipe/#align "Direct link to heading")
    
    if the old and new text have different length, you determine here, how they are layed on top of another.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/linear-wipe/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/linear-wipe/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    For each number, the random generator produces different results. If you have chosen "random" for Direction, you can try different numbers here to get different random directions.
    
    ##### Change for each Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/linear-wipe/#change-for-each-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option you get a different random values for each layer, if you apply the iExpression to several layers simultaneously

# Random Text Template | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#)

Random Text Template
====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/random_text_template_0-b8ada93dbbe9569126986f30045eb296.png)

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/random_text_template-34150ac8fe38e1da6d5e4c25496623cf.gif)

This iExpression replaces the source text of a text layer by a random text whose structure can be specified via a template. The template consists of fixed parts of the text as well as the place holders "%L", "%l", "%d", and "%b". The fixed parts remain unchanged in the generated random text whereas each occurence of %L is replaced by a random upper case letter, each occurrence of %l by a random lower case letter, each occurence of %n by a random number (0 to 9) and each occurrence of %b by a random binary number (0 or 1). Example: The template "Coordinates: (%n%n,%n%n)" can generate random texts like "Coordinates: (15,72)" or "Coordinates: (49,14)".

*   ##### Template[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#template "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the template which the random text is based on. The random text is obtained from the template by replacing any occurrence of %L,%l,%b,%n by some of the below specified characters. The remaining parts of the template are kept unchanged.
    
*   ##### Speed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#speed "Direct link to heading")
    
    at a value of 5, every 5 frames a new random text is generated. Set this to 0 if you don't want the text to change at all.
    
*   ##### Replacement Characters[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#replacement-characters "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### replace %L by[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#replace-l-by "Direct link to heading")
    
    specifies by which letters any occurence of %L in the template will be replaced. Among the letters specified here, for each %L one is randomly chosen.
    
    ##### replace %l by[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#replace-l-by-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    specifies by which letters any occurence of %l in the template will be replaced. Among the letters specified here, for each %l one is randomly chosen.
    
    ##### replace %n by[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#replace-n-by "Direct link to heading")
    
    specifies by which letters any occurence of %n in the template will be replaced. Among the letters specified here, for each %n one is randomly chosen.
    
    ##### replace %b by[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#replace-b-by "Direct link to heading")
    
    specifies by which letters any occurence of %b in the template will be replaced. Among the letters specified here, for each %b one is randomly chosen.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    Change this number arbitrarily to get another random text. For each number a different random text is generated.
    
    ##### Change for each Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text-template/#change-for-each-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option you get a different random text for each layer, if you apply the iExpression to several layers simultaneously

# Random Text | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text/#)

On this page

Random Text
===========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/screenshot_2019-11-13_10.54.51-0cd1d78ba23b1a716d50c6a09562325a.png)

iExpressions contains various different Random Text iExpressions.

These iExpression replace the source text of a text layer by a randomly generated text block of a given number of rows and columns (or a single line for the expressions without the word 'Block' in their name). Different kinds of text can be generated, e.g. binary numbers, numbers, or random strings using the letters of the alphabet.

### Variants[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text/#variants "Direct link to heading")

![Mutating Random Text Block created in After Effects with Expressions](<Base64-Image-Removed>)

#### Random Text Block Mutating[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text/#random-text-block-mutating "Direct link to heading")

This random text mutates over time, i.e. individual letters change spontaneously from time to time. The created animations are loopable, i.e. after a given number of frames, the text is again the same as it was at the first frame.

![Scrolling Random Text Block created in After Effects with Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/random_text_block_shifting-01f8113603841fc60e77f90d1bb55afd.gif)

#### Random Text Block Shifting[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text/#random-text-block-shifting "Direct link to heading")

The _Random Text Block Shifting_ allows to scroll though the text from left to right, up, down or in any other direction.

![Simple Random Text Block created in After Effects with Expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/random_text_block_simple-8b1aa1533a2e43e9dd8d09f14b4ea722.gif)

#### Random Text Block Simple[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text/#random-text-block-simple "Direct link to heading")

The _Random Text Block Simple_ replaces all letters at once every few frames (as specified in the _speed_ parameter). If you set the _speed_ to zero, it does not change at all. It computes faster than the other text blocks, so best use this variant if you don't need the text to change over time.

#### Single Line Variants[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-text/#single-line-variants "Direct link to heading")

For each _Random Text Block_ expression there also exists a corresponding expression without _Block_ in the name. If you just need a single line of text best use these single line variants, to get optimal performance.

![Line of Random Text created with After Effects expressions](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/random_text_shifting-e582365c50e2705812a0ac5d2d4b471b.gif)

# Vertical Align Text | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/vertical-align-text/#)

Vertical Align Text
===================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/vertical_align_text_0-750fc2a3e1693ae46f2955a9a6bdd147.png)

This iExpression offers a simple possibility to align a text vertically (top/middle/bottom) also if the text changes over time.

This is useful, if you have keyframes on the source text or if you want to replace the text - for example using scripts - and don't want to realign it manually each time.

To use the iExpression, you need to specify a maximum number of lines and the expression appends empty lines to the top and bottom of the text until this maximum number of lines is reached. By this, your text has always the same numer of lines and hence the same height, no matter what size the actual content is. Depending on the options you set, the original text is placed in the middle (adding the same number of empty lines to top and bottom), at the top (adding all empty lines at the bottom), or at the bottom (adding all empty lines at the top).

*   ##### Number of Lines[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/vertical-align-text/#number-of-lines "Direct link to heading")
    
    The maximum number of lines for the text. (Or to how many lines the text should be extended with empty lines).
    
*   ##### Alignment[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/vertical-align-text/#alignment "Direct link to heading")
    
    How the text should be aligned: center (higher): text in the middle, in case of doubt one more empty line at the bottom than at the top center (lower): text in the middle, in case of doubt one more empty line at the top than at the bottom top: text placed at the top, all empty lines below it bottom: text placed at the bottm, all empty lines above it
    
*   ##### Empty Lines Content[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/vertical-align-text/#empty-lines-content "Direct link to heading")
    
    Leave empty normally. For testing purposes, but here any letter you like to visualize the empty lines inserted by the iExpression.
    
*   ##### Longer Texts[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/vertical-align-text/#longer-texts "Direct link to heading")
    
    What to do when the actual text has more lines than specified in 'Number of Lines'

# Text Combiner | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner/#)

Text Combiner
=============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/text_combiner_0-50de6a82de06e0d53785b3bca2e00f96.png)

![Text Combiner expression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/text_combiner_0-7506441143d0c3c96842af8ab90256c3.gif)

With this iExpression you can combine two texts (a foreground and a background text) by placing the first on top of the other (i.e. the foreground preplaces some part of the background text). With this, you can, for example, embed some message into a randomly generated text block, or can create more complex text blocks by placing one random text block on top of another one.

*   ##### Background Text Block[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner/#background-text-block "Direct link to heading")
    
    Link this variable to the source text of the text layer containing the background text block.
    
*   ##### Foreground Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner/#foreground-text "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner/#text "Direct link to heading")
    
    The forground text that should be placed on top of the background text block. Can also be linked to a multi-line text block.
    
    ##### Position[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner/#position "Direct link to heading")
    
    In which column and row of the background the foreground text is inserted.
    
*   ##### Space=Transparent[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner/#spacetransparent "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option space characters in the foreground do not replace background characters.

# Text Combiner Multi | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#)

Text Combiner Multi
===================

*   [Aug 07, 2013](https://mamoworld.com/after-effects-expression/text-combiner-multi)
    
*   admin
*   [](https://mamoworld.com/after-effects-expression/text-combiner-multi)
    
*   [0 comments](https://mamoworld.com/after-effects-expression/text-combiner-multi)
    

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/text_combiner_multi_0-b437dfa4df3c191dc5d6a2a5b64c83da.png)

![Text Combiner Multi iExpression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/text_combiner-7506441143d0c3c96842af8ab90256c3.gif)

This iExpression works exactly as the 'Text Combiner' iExpression but allows to insert up to 4 different foreground texts into the background text.

*   ##### Background Text Block[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#background-text-block "Direct link to heading")
    
    Link this variable to the source text of the text layer containing the background text block.
    
*   ##### Foreground Text 1[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#foreground-text-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#text "Direct link to heading")
    
    The forground text that should be placed on top of the background text block. Can also be linked to a multi-line text block.
    
    ##### Position[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#position "Direct link to heading")
    
    In which column and row of the background the foreground text is inserted.
    
*   ##### Foreground Text 2[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#foreground-text-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#text-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    The forground text that should be placed on top of the background text block. Can also be linked to a multi-line text block.
    
    ##### Position[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#position-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    In which column and row of the background the foreground text is inserted.
    
*   ##### Foreground Text 3[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#foreground-text-3 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#text-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    The forground text that should be placed on top of the background text block. Can also be linked to a multi-line text block.
    
    ##### Position[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#position-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    In which column and row of the background the foreground text is inserted.
    
*   ##### Foreground Text 4[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#foreground-text-4 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#text-3 "Direct link to heading")
    
    The forground text that should be placed on top of the background text block. Can also be linked to a multi-line text block.
    
    ##### Position[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#position-3 "Direct link to heading")
    
    In which column and row of the background the foreground text is inserted.
    
*   ##### Space=Transparent[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/text-combiner-multi/#spacetransparent "Direct link to heading")

# Counter Time | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#)

Counter Time
============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/counter_time_0-8a8aa8bc472fbc20e3c2bef64dd3472f.png)

With this iExpression you can create a time string and animate it over time. The date can be formatted freely and additional text can be added before and after the time itself. Simply link "Link to Counter Value" to a slider control and keyframe it to values between 0 and 100. Then you specify which time correpsonds to the value 0 and which one to 100. The iExpression figures out the time for all other values accordingly.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/counter_time-46d684f7c8f72155e854c1292bf08910.gif)

*   ##### Link to Counter Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#link-to-counter-value "Direct link to heading")
    
    link this to a slider control which you can keyframe to control the time shown by the counter
    
*   ##### Time for Value 0 (hⓂ️s:ms)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#time-for-value-0-h%E2%93%9C%EF%B8%8Fsms "Direct link to heading")
    
    time that should be shown when the linked slider control has the value 0. Format - hours:minutes:seconds:milliseconds e.g. 3:20:12:344 = 3 hours, 20 min, 12 sec und 344 ms 10:34:12 = 10 hours, 34 min and 12 sec 10 = 10 hours
    
*   ##### Time for Value 100 (hⓂ️s:ms)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#time-for-value-100-h%E2%93%9C%EF%B8%8Fsms "Direct link to heading")
    
    time that should be shown when the linked slider control has the value 100. Format - hours:minutes:seconds:milliseconds e.g. 3:20:12:344 = 3 hours, 20 min, 12 sec and 344 ms 10:34:12 = 10 hours, 34 min and 12 sec 10 = 10 hours
    
*   ##### Days between 0 and 100[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#days-between-0-and-100 "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows that the start and end time are not on the same day. The counter then does not directly count from the start to the end time, but completes several 24 hours loops before arriving at the end time.
    
*   ##### Time Format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#time-format "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Time Format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#time-format-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines, how exactly the time should look like. When the time string is created, this text is taken and symbols %h, %m, %s and %l are replaced by hours, minutes, seconds and milliseconds of the time, respecively. Additionaly %a is available to show am or pm. You can arrange the symbols freely (and also leave some out if you want, for example, no milliseconds). Inbetween the symbols you can add arbitrary other text.
    
    ##### Hour Format (%h)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#hour-format-h "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the hours are formatted
    
    ##### Minutes Format (%m)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#minutes-format-m "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the minutes are formatted
    
    ##### Seconds Format (%s)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#seconds-format-s "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the seconds are formatted
    
    ##### Milliseconds Format (%l)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#milliseconds-format-l "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the milliseconds are formatted
    
    ##### am / pm Format (%a)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#am--pm-format-a "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the a.m. or p.m. string is formatted
    
*   ##### Surrounding Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#surrounding-text "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text Before[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#text-before "Direct link to heading")
    
    an optional text that is inserted before the time itself
    
    ##### Text After[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-time/#text-after "Direct link to heading")
    
    an optional text that is inserted after the time itself

# Scroll Text Terminal | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/scroll-text-terminal/#)

Scroll Text Terminal
====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/scroll_text_terminal_0-4a74f03cdd81fe86601ba7918cb0867e.png)

This iExpression can be used to simulate the output of a computer terminal. The text is faded it character by character and scrolls such that only a given number of lines is visible. Also, a (optional) blinking cursor symbol is attached.

*   ##### Link to Completion[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/scroll-text-terminal/#link-to-completion "Direct link to heading")
    
    How far the text is already scrolled (0 to 100%). Link it to a slider to keyframe it.
    
*   ##### Visible Lines[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/scroll-text-terminal/#visible-lines "Direct link to heading")
    
    How many lines of text are visible simultaneously.
    
*   ##### Cursor[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/scroll-text-terminal/#cursor "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Cursor Symbol[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/scroll-text-terminal/#cursor-symbol "Direct link to heading")
    
    The caret symbol that is blinking at the end of the text. Empty for no caret.
    
    ##### Blink Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/scroll-text-terminal/#blink-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    controls, how fast the cursor is blinking. 12 means it stays 12 frames visible and then 12 frames invisible.

# Random Letter Wipe | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#)

Random Letter Wipe
==================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/random_letter_wipe_0-f1e36cdc12d0cc6d4cf4647b8e7d71e9.png)

Creates a transition from one text to another one by replacing the first text letter by letter with quickly changing random letters that finally become the second text. This iExpression is applied to the source text property of a text layer. Create for each text a keyframe and the iExpression computes automatically the transition between the keyframes. If some text should remain unchanged for some time, simply create two keyframes with the same text at the beginning and end of this region.

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/text_transition_random_letter_wipe-2fe637209bc88d33dca2f4a6e7cd1245.gif)

*   ##### Random String Width[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#random-string-width "Direct link to heading")
    
    How many letters are replaced by random letters in parallel.
    
*   ##### Random Letter Duration[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#random-letter-duration "Direct link to heading")
    
    For how many frames each single random letter stays until it is replaced by a new one.
    
*   ##### Direction[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#direction "Direct link to heading")
    
    the order in which the letters are replaced.
    
*   ##### Align[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#align "Direct link to heading")
    
    if the old and new text have different length, you determine here, how they are layed on top of another.
    
*   ##### Alphabet[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#alphabet "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Alphabet[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#alphabet-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    which symbols to use for the random letters (input = letters of the text itself, binary e.g. 100101010, numbers e.g. 9873548923, hex e.g. 19af21, HEX z.B. 19AF21, letters e.g. asfgdsdfg, LeTTers e.g. AlfDfaSA; LETTERS e.g. AGUAFZTCTAS)
    
    ##### Custom Alphabet[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#custom-alphabet "Direct link to heading")
    
    Those characters are used, when the alphabet 'Custom' is choosen. If letters occur more than once in the alphabet, they are used more frequently, e.g. for 'aaabc' the letter 'a' occurs 3 times as often in the random text as 'b' or 'c'.
    
    ##### Use Spaces[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#use-spaces "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option the generated text also contains empty spaces.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    For each number, the random generator produces different results. If you have chosen "random" for Direction, you can try different numbers here to get different random directions.
    
    ##### Change for each Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/random-letter-wipe/#change-for-each-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option you get a different random values for each layer, if you apply the iExpression to several layers simultaneously

# Distribute 3D Grid | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#)

Distribute 3D Grid
==================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_3d_grid-af5a40901913f24b9898d181ab038ea5.png)

With this iExpression you can distribute 3D layers on a 3D grid. Just specify the first layer and all other layers that you apply the iExpression to, will be placed next to this layer in the given distance. You can still move layers away from their grid position using their keyframe values or the wiggle parameters.

*   ##### First Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#first-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### First Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#first-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first layer. This layer is the upper, left, topmost corner of the grid and its position determines the position of the grid.
    
    ##### Position[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#position "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the position of the first layer in the grid.
    
*   ##### Distance[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#distance "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the distance between neighbouring layers in the grid.
    
*   ##### Grid Size[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#grid-size "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes how many layers should be placed in one row, one column, and behind each other.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to move each layer away from its position in the grid using keyframes. In order to be exactly placed at its position in the grid, a layer then must have exactly the same keyframed position as the first layer of the grid. If the position is different, the difference is used as an offset to the grid position.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its position in the grid with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its grid position. To create a little less regular but still not moving grid, set the frequency to 0 and the amplitude to some value greater 0.
    
*   ##### Layer Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#layer-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-grid/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to delete the first rows of the grid (in x, y, or z direction)and insert them again behind the last rows.

# News Ticker | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/news-ticker/#)

News Ticker
===========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/news_ticker-1097bfcfff9c25be42848a1255129752.png)

This iExpression makes the source text of a text layer scroll through the screen from left to right (or right to left). If the text consists of more than one row, each row is interpreted as a separate news and between the news a customizable separator text can be inserted.

*   ##### Separator[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/news-ticker/#separator "Direct link to heading")
    
    is a string that is inserted between the different lines of the original text (i.e. the different news messages).
    
*   ##### Speed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/news-ticker/#speed "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines how fast the text moves. A value of 10 means every minute 10 new letters become visible. For negative values the text moves from left to right otherwise it moves from right to left.
    
*   ##### Length[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/news-ticker/#length "Direct link to heading")
    
    How long the visible text should be (number of characters). To make the entire text visible, you can also set the length to 0.
    
*   ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/news-ticker/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to start the news ticker not with the beginning of the first news message but at any position in the text.

# Evolution | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#)

Evolution
=========

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/evolution_0-fa9a07d7223a1e1cfae4b44f7fcefd71.png)

Creates a transition from one text to another one by step by step rewriting the first text in the second one. During the rewrite, as many characters as possible are preserved, i.e. as few letters as possible are deleted or inserted. This iExpression is applied to the source text property of a text layer. Create for each text a keyframe and the iExpression computes automatically the transition between the keyframes. If some text should remain unchanged for some time, simply create two keyframes with the same text at the beginning and end of this region.

![Text Transition Evolution](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/text_transition_evolution-a697c88a9e05f69885ac8cea4724a345.gif)

*   ##### Edit Direction[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#edit-direction "Direct link to heading")
    
    in which direction the text is rewritten
    
*   ##### Edit Order[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#edit-order "Direct link to heading")
    
    For "replace - delete - insert" the rewrite process first performs all replacements of letters, then all deletions and finally all insertions.
    
*   ##### Allow Replace[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#allow-replace "Direct link to heading")
    
    when disabled, letters can only be deleted and inserted, but not be replaced by other letters.
    
*   ##### Advanced[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#advanced "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Output[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#output "Direct link to heading")
    
    "alignment" produces a status output that explains which letter of the first text is rewritten to which letter of the second text.
    
    ##### Force Match[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#force-match "Direct link to heading")
    
    Allows to fine-tune which letter of the first text becomes which letter of the second text. The value \[10,5\] enforces, for example, that the 10th letter of the first text becomes the 5th letter of the second text. For \[0,0\] the option is ignored.
    
    ##### Force Match[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#force-match-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Allows to fine-tune which letter of the first text becomes which letter of the second text. The value \[10,5\] enforces, for example, that the 10th letter of the first text becomes the 5th letter of the second text. For \[0,0\] the option is ignored.
    
    ##### Force Match[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#force-match-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    Allows to fine-tune which letter of the first text becomes which letter of the second text. The value \[10,5\] enforces, for example, that the 10th letter of the first text becomes the 5th letter of the second text. For \[0,0\] the option is ignored.
    
    ##### Force Match Lines[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#force-match-lines "Direct link to heading")
    
    for mutiline texts: considers each line separately, i.e. the first line of the first text becomes the first line of the second text and so on. ATTENTION: deaktivating this option might cause the iExpression to become very slow.
    
    ##### Scoring[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#scoring "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Insert/Delete Cost[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#insertdelete-cost "Direct link to heading")
    
    Imagine you have to rewrite a text into another text and each insertion, deletion or replacement of a letter costs something. That's exactly what this iExpression does - and it does it in the way that costs as little as possible. Here you can specify how much an insertion or deletion of a letter costs. The more expensive it is, the more the iExpression will try to avoid insertions and deletions and use replacement operations instead.
    
    ##### Replace Cost[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#replace-cost "Direct link to heading")
    
    Imagine you have to rewrite a text into another text and each insertion, deletion or replacement of a letter costs something. That's exactly what this iExpression does - and it does it in the way that costs as little as possible. Here you can specify how much the replacement of a letter costs. The more expensive it is, the more the iExpression will try to avoid replacements and use insertions or deletions instead.
    
    ##### Word Boundary Match Bonus[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#word-boundary-match-bonus "Direct link to heading")
    
    As described at the parameters "Insert/Delete Cost" and "Replace Cost", this iExpression works with a cost model and tries to rewrite a text "as cheap as possible" into another text. Here you can specify that the iExpression may subtract a bonus from the costs, whenever it manages to preserve a space character that it does not delete or overwrite. The higher (i.e. more negative) this cost bonus is, the more the iExpression will try to preserve spaces, e.g. it will try to turn each single word of the first text into a single word of the second text instead of, for example, merging two words of the first text into one word of the second text.
    
    ##### Line Boundary Match Bonus[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#line-boundary-match-bonus "Direct link to heading")
    
    This option works exactly like "Word Boundary Match Bonus" but does not give a bonus for preserved space symbols, but for preserved line beaks. This is only relevant if "Force Match Lines" is disabled.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    For each number, the random generator produces different results. If you have chosen "random" for Edit Direction, you can try different numbers here to get different random directions.
    
    ##### Change for each Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/evolution/#change-for-each-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option, for Edit Direction = "random" you get for each layer a different random direction. Deactivate this option, if the randomness should behave exactly identical on all duplicates of a layer.
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Distribute 3D Circle and Spiral | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#)

Distribute 3D Circle and Spiral
===============================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_3d_circle_and_spiral-fb837149b4717c7a9c1efd93d5b9fd09.png)

With this iExpression you can place 3D layers in a circle. Variants like ellipses, spirals or arcs of circles are also possible. This iExpression works exactly as the 2D variant but offers the additional parameter z Offset for the first and last layer to create spirals that are stretched in z direction. In order to orient the layers to look at the middle of the circle, apply the "Look At Line 3D" iExpression to their orientation and choose as first point of the line the center of the sphere and as second point an arbitrary point exactly behind or before it (e.g. the center with a value of 100 added to its z value). If you want the layers to rotate along the circle but do not look at the middle, apply the "Look At Point 2D" iExpression instead to the z rotation and use as "Look At" point (the x and y value of) the center. If you want to move or rotate the circle, its best to set the center to (0,0,0), then create a new NULL object, make it 3D and parent the layers on the circle surface to this NULL. During the parenting keep the alt or option key pressed. Then the position of the NULL becomes the center of the cylinder and you can move or rotate it using the transform controls of the NULL.

*   ##### Center[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#center "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the center of the circle (or of the ellipse, spiral,...)
    
*   ##### First Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#first-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first layer that should be placed in the circle.
    
    ##### Angle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#angle "Direct link to heading")
    
    this angle gives the direction where (relative to the center) the first layer is placed. 0 degree means above the center (12 o clock), 90 degree to the right of if (3 o clock), 180 degree below it (6 o clock) etc.
    
    ##### Radius[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#radius "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the distance that the first layer has to the center. For circles, this value is identical in both dimensions, for ellipsis you can choose different values in x and y direction.
    
    ##### z Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#z-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    moves the first point in z direction (relative to center)
    
*   ##### Last Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#last-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#layer-name-or-index-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the last layer that should be placed in the circle.
    
    ##### Angle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#angle-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    this angle gives the direction where (relative to the center) the last layer is placed. 0 degree means above the center (12 o clock), 90 degree to the right of if (3 o clock), 180 degree below it (6 o clock) etc.
    
    ##### use Radius of First Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#use-radius-of-first-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option the last layer uses the radius of the first layer (and ignored its own radius). Use this to create circles and ellipses; for spirals the radius of the first and last layer must be different.
    
    ##### Radius[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#radius-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the distance that the first layer has to the center. For circles, this value is identical in both dimensions, for ellipsis you can choose different values in x and y direction. For spirals this parameter should differ from the respective parameter for the first layer.
    
    ##### z Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#z-offset-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    moves the last point in z direction (relative to center)
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    adds the keyframed value to the position on the circle. Hence, the layer is only placed exactly on the circle when the keyframed position is (0,0). Other values cause a corresponding offset and allow to animate the position relative to its placement on the circle.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its position in the circle with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its position on the cirle. To create a little less regular but still not moving circle, set the frequency to 0 and the amplitude to some value greater 0.
    
    ##### Radial Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#radial-wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layers stay exactly on the circle while they wiggle. In other words, only the angle is wiggled, but not the radius.
    
*   ##### Layer Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#layer-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-circle-and-spiral/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    with an Offset of one, the first layer is removed from the beginning of the arc and added again at the end behind the last layer. For an offset of 2 this happens for the first two layers etc.

# Appear Disappear per Character | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/appear-disappear-character/#)

Appear Disappear per Character
==============================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/appear_disappear_per_character_0-363a0165b6168615bb72273c5aa33d48.png)

Creates a transition from one text to another one by first deleting the first text letter by letter and then inserting the second text letter by letter. This iExpression is applied to the source text property of a text layer. Create for each text a keyframe and the iExpression computes automatically the transition between the keyframes. If some text should remain unchanged for some time, simply create two keyframes with the same text at the beginning and end of this region.

![Appear Disappear per Character](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/text_transition_appear_disappear-0b549d0d18bb53f23214df64c3f4952b.gif)

*   ##### Disappear Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/appear-disappear-character/#disappear-type "Direct link to heading")
    
    the order in which the letters of the old text disappear.
    
*   ##### Appear Type[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/appear-disappear-character/#appear-type "Direct link to heading")
    
    the order in which the letters of the new text appear.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/appear-disappear-character/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/appear-disappear-character/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    For each number, the random generator produces different results. If you have chosen "random" for Appear Typ or Disappear Type, you can try different numbers here to get different random orders.
    
    ##### Change for each Layer[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/appear-disappear-character/#change-for-each-layer "Direct link to heading")
    
    With this option you get a different random values for each layer, if you apply the iExpression to several layers simultaneously

# Distribute 3D Line | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#)

Distribute 3D Line
==================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_3d_line-654033e8d5f369048fc50ff6a3478049.png)

With this iExpression you can place 3d layers in a straight line in 3d space.

*   ##### First Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#first-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first layer that should be placed on the line.
    
*   ##### Last Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#last-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the last layer that should be placed on the line.
    
*   ##### Starting Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#starting-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the starting point of the line on which the layers should be distributed.
    
*   ##### Ending Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#ending-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the ending point of the line on which the layers should be distributed.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    adds the keyframed value to the position on the line. Hence, all layers except the first and the last one are then only placed exactly on the line when their keyframed position is (0,0,0). Other values cause a corresponding offset and allow to animate the position relative to its placement on the line.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its position on the line with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its position on the line. To create a little less regular but still not moving line, set the frequency to 0 and the amplitude to some value greater 0.
    
    ##### Wiggle On Line[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#wiggle-on-line "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layers stay exactly on the line while they wiggle. In other words, it only wiggles in the direction of the line.
    
*   ##### Layer Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#layer-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-line/#offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    with an offset of one, the first layer is removed from the beginning of the line and added again at the end behind the last layer. For an offset of 2 this happens for the first two layers etc.

# Distribute 3D Random | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#)

Distribute 3D Random
====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_3d_random-14d73910f6e5d1f0aed80a3c697fc69a.png)

With this iExpression you can distribute 3D layers (or other 3D properties) randomly. You can still move layers away from their random position using their keyframe values or the wiggle parameters.

*   ##### Minimum Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#minimum-value "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the minimum of the possible random values. Note that if you use the wiggle or keyframe offset options, the value could become smaller that this.
    
*   ##### Maximum Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#maximum-value "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the maximum of the possible random values. Note that if you use the wiggle or keyframe offset options, the value could become larger that this.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    adds the keyframed value of the property to the random value. This allows you to move the property away from its keyframed value using keyframes.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its random position with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its random position when it wiggles.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    can be modified to get other random values.
    
    ##### Change Over Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-random/#change-over-time "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to get a new random value for each frame.

# Look At Line 2D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-2d/#)

Look At Line 2D
===============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/look_at_line_2d-61a5f0559fca1dd114fc50b3f9962543.png)

This iExpression is applied to the rotation of a 2D layer. It rotates the layer such that it always "looks" towards a given line. Each layer looks at the point of the line that is closest to it. In other words, the direction in which the layers look is perpendicular to the direction of the line. The line has an infinite length and is given by two (arbitrary) points on the line. The rotation can still be adjusted using keyframes as usual.

*   ##### Line Point 1[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-2d/#line-point-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the first point on the line at which the layer should "look".
    
*   ##### Line Point 2[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-2d/#line-point-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the second point on the line at which the layer should "look".
    
*   ##### Flip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-2d/#flip "Direct link to heading")
    
    flips the rotation such that the layer does not look at the line but looks away from it (i.e. turns its back on it).

# Distribute 3D Sphere Random | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#)

Distribute 3D Sphere Random
===========================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_3d_sphere_random-98e75a1d6aad511910de5c0d7aa20e37.png)

With this iExpression you can distribute 3D layers (or other 3D properties) randomly on the surface of a sphere or ellipsoid. You can restrict the layers also to be placed only on the upper or lower half of the sphere. You can still move layers away from their random position using their keyframe values or the wiggle parameters. In order to orient the layers correctly on the sphere surface, apply the "Look At Point 3D" iExpression to their orientation and choose as "Look At" point the center of the sphere. If you want to move or rotate the sphere, its best to set the center to (0,0,0), then create a new NULL object, make it 3D and parent the layers on the sphere surface to this NULL. During the parenting, keep the alt or option key pressed. Then the position of the NULL becomes the center of the sphere and you can move or rotate it using the transform controls of the NULL.

*   ##### Center[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#center "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the center of the sphere.
    
*   ##### Radius[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#radius "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the radius of the sphere. For spheres choose the same value for x, y, and z component; otherwise ellipsoids are generated.
    
*   ##### Restrict To[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#restrict-to "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to restrict the placement of the random positions to the upper or lower half of the sphere.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    adds the keyframed value of the property to the random value. This allows you to move the property away from its keyframed value using keyframes.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its random position with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its random position when it wiggles.
    
    ##### Wiggle on Surface[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#wiggle-on-surface "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layers stay exactly on the sphere surface while they wiggle.
    
*   ##### Randomness[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#randomness "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Random Seed[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#random-seed "Direct link to heading")
    
    can be modified to get other random values.
    
    ##### Change Over Time[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-random/#change-over-time "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to get a new random value for each frame.

# Look At Line 3D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-3d/#)

Look At Line 3D
===============

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/look_at_line_3d-f58df9317685e6b58f21fe746de8e6a0.png)

This iExpression is applied to the orientation of a 3D layer. It rotates the layer such that it always "looks" towards a given line. Each layer looks at the point of the line that is closest to it. In other words, the direction in which the layers look is perpendicular to the direction of the line. The line has an infinite length and is given by two (arbitrary) points on the line. The rotation can still be adjusted using keyframes as usual. You can, for example, align layers on the surface of a cylinder by choosing as line the axis in the middle of the cylinder.

*   ##### Line Point 1[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-3d/#line-point-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the first point on the line at which the layer should "look".
    
*   ##### Line Point 2[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-3d/#line-point-2 "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the second point on the line at which the layer should "look".
    
*   ##### Flip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-3d/#flip "Direct link to heading")
    
    flips the rotation such that the layer does not look at the line but looks away from it (i.e. turns its back on it).
    
*   ##### Look From[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-3d/#look-from "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Look From[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-3d/#look-from-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    if you choose 'other point' here, the layer looks not from its own position but as if it was located at some other position. If you want, for example, that the layers looks in exactly the same direction as another layer which uses this iExpression, then you can simply link this other layer's position to 'other point'.
    
    ##### Other Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-line-3d/#other-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the point at which the layer should "look" when the option 'other point' has been choosen for 'Look From'.

# Look At Point 3D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-3d/#)

Look At Point 3D
================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/look_at_point_3d-46721a30a885aad40a639d56ac460be9.png)

This iExpression is applied to the orientation of a 3D layer. It orients the layer such that it always "looks" towards the given point. The orientation can still be adjusted using keyframes as usual.

*   ##### Look At[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-3d/#look-at "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the point at which the layer should "look".
    
*   ##### Flip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-3d/#flip "Direct link to heading")
    
    flips the orientation such that the layer does not look at the point but looks away from it (i.e. turns its back on it).
    
*   ##### Look From[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-3d/#look-from "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Look From[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-3d/#look-from-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    if you choose 'other point' here, the layer looks not from its own position but as if it was located at some other position. If you want, for example, that the layers looks in exactly the same direction as another layer which uses this iExpression, then you can simply link this other layer's position to 'other point'.
    
    ##### Other Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-3d/#other-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the point at which the layer should "look" when the option 'other point' has been choosen for 'Look From'.

# Distribute 3D Sphere Evenly | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#)

Distribute 3D Sphere Evenly
===========================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/distribute_3d_sphere_evenly-7c9c7330a60dc0fcc7356b2808bd82da.png)

With this iExpression you can distribute 3D layers (or other 3D properties) evenly on the surface of a sphere or ellipsoid. You can still move layers away from their random position using their keyframe values or the wiggle parameters. In order to orient the layers correctly on the sphere surface, apply the "Look At Point 3D" iExpression to their orientation and choose as "Look At" point the center of the sphere. If you want to move or rotate the sphere, its best to set the center to (0,0,0), then create a new NULL object, make it 3D and parent the layers on the sphere surface to this NULL. During the parenting, keep the alt or option key pressed. Then the position of the NULL becomes the center of the sphere and you can move or rotate it using the transform controls of the NULL.

*   ##### First Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#first-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first layer that should be placed on the surface of the sphere.
    
*   ##### Last Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#last-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the last layer that should be placed on the surface of the sphere.
    
*   ##### Center[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#center "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the center of the sphere.
    
*   ##### Radius[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#radius "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the radius of the sphere. For spheres choose the same value for x, y, and z component; otherwise ellipsoids are generated.
    
*   ##### Restrict To[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#restrict-to "Direct link to heading")
    
    allows to restrict the placement of the random positions to the upper or lower half of the sphere.
    
*   ##### Use Keyframe Value as Offset[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#use-keyframe-value-as-offset "Direct link to heading")
    
    adds the keyframed value of the property to the random value. This allows you to move the property away from its keyframed value using keyframes.
    
*   ##### Wiggle[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#wiggle "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Frequency[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#frequency "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layer wiggle around its random position with the given frequency. If you don't want it to move at all, set the frequency to 0.
    
    ##### Amplitude[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#amplitude "Direct link to heading")
    
    denotes up to how much the layer may move away from its random position when it wiggles.
    
    ##### Wiggle on Surface[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/distribute-3d-sphere-evenly/#wiggle-on-surface "Direct link to heading")
    
    makes the layers stay exactly on the sphere surface while they wiggle.

# Simple Link 2D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/simple-link-2d/#)

On this page

Simple Link 2D
==============

![](<Base64-Image-Removed>)

This iExpression links a 2D property to another 1D, 2D, or 3D property. As in any other link of iExpressions, you can choose in the options of "Link to" many details of the link, e.g. whether to link to the actual value or to its speed and which component should be linked to the x and y component, respectively.

*   ##### Link to[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/simple-link-2d/#link-to "Direct link to heading")
    
    Don't put a number here. Use the link button to link this to some property.
