# Layer Distance Link | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#)

Layer Distance Link
===================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/layer_distance_link_ui-cd88cbe81e90daa6104111f30811f283.png)

This iExpression calculates the distance between two 2d or 3d layers. It can also calculate the distance only in a particular direction.

*   ##### First Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#first-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the first of the two layers whose distance should be measured
    
*   ##### Second Layer (Name or Index)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#second-layer-name-or-index "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the name or index of the second of the two layers whose distance should be measured
    
*   ##### Compute[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#compute "Direct link to heading")
    
    Determines whether the total distance or only the distance in a specific direction should be calculated.
    
*   ##### Options[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#options "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Allow Negative Values[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#allow-negative-values "Direct link to heading")
    
    Allows negative distances in order to distinguish different directions (only if distance is measured in x- or y-direction).
    
    ##### Scale[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#scale "Direct link to heading")
    
    Allows to scale the computed distance value.
    
    ##### Enable Clipping[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#enable-clipping "Direct link to heading")
    
    Clips the computed distance value such that it always stays between the given minimal and maximal distance.
    
    ##### Min Distance[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#min-distance "Direct link to heading")
    
    If the computed distance is smaller than this value, this value will be returned.
    
    ##### Max Distance[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/linking/layer-distance-link/#max-distance "Direct link to heading")
    
    If the computed distance is larger than this value, this value will be returned.

# Counter Date and Time | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#)

Counter Date and Time
=====================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/counter_date_and_time_0-08af1145c11a080298fc3b98c7f2e22c.png)

With this iExpression you can create a date string including time information and animate it over time. The date and time can be formatted freely. Simply link "Link to Counter Value" to a slider control and keyframe it to values between 0 and 100. Then you specify which date and time correpsonds to the value 0 and which one to 100. The iExpression figures out the date and time for all other values accordingly.

![Counter Date and Time expression](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/counter_date_and_time-815ddf942a429e2b558099a3904d8649.gif)

*   ##### Link to Counter Value[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#link-to-counter-value "Direct link to heading")
    
    link this to a slider control which you can keyframe to control the date (including the time) shown by the counter
    
*   ##### Date for Value 0[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#date-for-value-0 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Date[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#date "Direct link to heading")
    
    date that should be shown when the linked slider control has the value 0. Enter the date with month names as words,e.g. "6 January 1972" or "Nov 23 2012"
    
    ##### Time (hⓂ️s:ms)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#time-h%E2%93%9C%EF%B8%8Fsms "Direct link to heading")
    
    time that should be shown when the linked slider control has the value 0. Format - hours:minutes:seconds:milliseconds e.g. 3:20:12:344 = 3 hours, 20 min, 12 sec und 344 ms 10:34:12 = 10 hours, 34 min and 12 sec 10 = 10 hours
    
*   ##### Date for Value 100[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#date-for-value-100 "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Date[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#date-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    date that should be shown when the linked slider control has the value 100. Enter the date with month names as words,e.g. "6 January 1972" or "Nov 23 2012"
    
    ##### Time (hⓂ️s:ms)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#time-h%E2%93%9C%EF%B8%8Fsms-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    time that should be shown when the linked slider control has the value 100. Format - hours:minutes:seconds:milliseconds e.g. 3:20:12:344 = 3 hours, 20 min, 12 sec and 344 ms 10:34:12 = 10 hours, 34 min and 12 sec 10 = 10 hours
    
*   ##### Date Format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#date-format "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Date Format[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#date-format-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    determines, how exactly the date should look like. When the date string is created, this text is taken and symbols %W, %D, %D and %Y are replaced by the day of the week, day of the month, month, and year, respectively. You can arrange the symbols freely (and also leave some out if you want, for example, no weekday). Inbetween the symbols you can add arbitrary other text.
    
    ##### Year Format (%Y)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#year-format-y "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the year is formatted
    
    ##### Month Format (%M)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#month-format-m "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the month is formatted
    
    ##### Day Format (%D)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#day-format-d "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the day of the month is formatted
    
    ##### Weekday Format (%W)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#weekday-format-w "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the weekday is formatted
    
    ##### Month Names[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#month-names "Direct link to heading")
    
    Here you can translate the name of each month into your language
    
    ##### Weekday Names[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#weekday-names "Direct link to heading")
    
    Here you can translate each day of the week into your language
    
    ##### Hour Format (%h)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#hour-format-h "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the hours are formatted
    
    ##### Minutes Format (%m)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#minutes-format-m "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the minutes are formatted
    
    ##### Seconds Format (%s)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#seconds-format-s "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the seconds are formatted
    
    ##### Milliseconds Format (%l)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#milliseconds-format-l "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the milliseconds are formatted
    
    ##### am / pm Format (%a)[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#am--pm-format-a "Direct link to heading")
    
    how the a.m. or p.m. string is formatted
    
*   ##### Surrounding Text[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#surrounding-text "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Text Before[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#text-before "Direct link to heading")
    
    an optional text that is inserted before the date itself
    
    ##### Text After[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/source-text/counter-date-and-time/#text-after "Direct link to heading")
    
    an optional text that is inserted after the date itself
    

  
  
  
Download at aescripts.com

# Look At Point 2D | Mamoworld Docs

[Skip to main content](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-2d/#)

Look At Point 2D
================

![](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/assets/images/look_at_point_2d-054ab97c7408572fce8c2c0ac6bfe974.png)

This iExpression is applied to the rotation of a 2D layer. It rotates the layer such that it always "looks" towards the given point. The rotation can still be adjusted using keyframes as usual.

*   ##### Look At[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-2d/#look-at "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the point at which the layer should "look".
    
*   ##### Flip[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-2d/#flip "Direct link to heading")
    
    flips the rotation such that the layer does not look at the point but looks away from it (i.e. turns its back on it).
    
*   ##### Look From[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-2d/#look-from "Direct link to heading")
    
    ##### Look From[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-2d/#look-from-1 "Direct link to heading")
    
    if you choose 'other point' here, the layer looks not from its own position but as if it was located at some other position. If you want, for example, that the layers looks in exactly the same direction as another layer which uses this iExpression, then you can simply link this other layer's position to 'other point'.
    
    ##### Other Point[​](https://docs.mamoworld.com/iexpressions/layer-placement/look-point-2d/#other-point "Direct link to heading")
    
    is the point at which the layer should "look" when the option 'other point' has been choosen for 'Look From'.
